# Introduction to AIRIS

The modern customer journey is non-linear, omnichannel, and cyclical. AIRIS was created out of the need to understand the customer experience by being truly channel and platform agnostic. AIRIS pioneered individual-level customer analytics even before businesses identified the challenges with nonlinear customer journeys and fragmented product engagement.

The platform tracks user behaviors across websites, through email, social media, paid media, sales, technical support, help desk, video, and most importantly, the engagement on the product/service itself.

AIRIS's journey analytics breaks down the data silos of mobile, app, product, or web analytics, and combines them with other essential touchpoints to help you see the world as your customers do.

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

[What is AIRIS: Product Capabilities](/docs/what-is-airis-product-capabilities)

# Activity Reports

![](https://files.readme.io/45da702-Image_2019-06-05_at_6.13.40_PM.png)

## 

Overview

Activity reports allow you to see a list of user activities, for example, if you want to see a list of new sign-ups and orders for a particular day.

* * *

## 

Creating an Activity report

To create a new Activity report go to **Activity** , then click **\+ New Activity Report**.

![](https://files.readme.io/6a7731c-activity-add-report.png)

Next, complete the following settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed By
  * Events
  * Columns



### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. The starting and ending dates for the timeframe can be set in the following ways:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.



### 

Performed By

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. Either select an existing segment or create a new segment by clicking the **+**. More info on how to create a Segment can be found here: [Segments](doc:segments).

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> AIRIS supports combining segmentation conditions using the **AND** and **OR** operators. To switch the operator, click on the operator name.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/72170ae-report-operator-selection.gif)

### 

Events

This is the event you want to analyze. You can select any event that you are tracking and add constraints to further filter the event.

![974](https://files.readme.io/7d14c34-Image_2019-06-05_at_6.05.13_PM.png)

Select your event, then add a constraint. Here we want to see all the songs that were played by the artist "Queen."

### 

Columns

The report columns determine how results will be grouped. For example, you may want certain information displayed, such as emails, product names, total amounts, and invoice numbers.

* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Now that you've built your report, you can share it with your team. You can do this by clicking on the three vertical dots which will open an options' menu for exporting, sharing, tagging, pinning, duplicating, and reloading your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/ac5aaf0-report-share-export-snapshot.png)

### 

Downloading

You can download reports in various formats, including CSV, PDF, and HTML.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, e.g. 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, 20 seconds.

### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to share it for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu and select what team you want to share your report with.

__Updated 11 months ago

* * *

# Global Filters

## 

Overview

Global filters can hide specific events from being used in reports or accessed in AIRIS.

### 

Limitations

  * Global filters are only accessible by admin users.
  * To avoid negative impacts on reporting performance, avoid setting the **Filter Out Events** setting to [data store](doc:data-augmentation-data-stores#setting-up-data-stores) event data.
  * To avoid data discrepancies between reports and user profiles, avoid setting the **Filter Out Events** setting to a **Property update** event.



* * *

## 

How to use Global Filters

Select the event and add any constraints. In this case, we're filtering out `page_viewed` events where the URL contains `"/admin/"`. After applying the filters, events matching the constraints will be hidden and filtered out from the report results.

![](https://files.readme.io/bb430dc-Screen_Shot_2024-03-05_at_5.18.56_PM.png)

# AdWords Auto-Tagging

Letting AdWords and AIRIS work together on campaign reporting with utm data.

Google AdWord's auto-tagging makes tracking your AdWords campaigns very easy! But in order to allow it to work with analytics solutions other than Google Analytics, you will have to do a few extra steps. Luckily, doing these steps will also give you some extra power and customizability in how you define and report on your campaigns.

Some concepts here may require some familiarity with how urls work and specifically, the query part of the url.

## 

Configuring Auto-Tagging: the Shared Library

AdWords has a section in its interface called the "Shared Library." This tab contains account-level settings and defaults that can be used in multiple campaigns. To set up UTM Auto-Tagging to work with AIRIS, you need to tell AdWords to send the campaign data in the universal `utm_` format, rather than encoded in the `gclid` that only Google Analytics can read. Here's how:

### 

Instructions

  1. Navigate to the Shared Library, and then to the "URL options." Note the URL options are not available from manager accounts, only on an individual ad account level.

![](https://files.readme.io/95ed5c6-69cd9a4-AdWords_Shared_Library.png)

  2. Turn on Auto-Tagging by clicking the edit button, and selecting the check box. This will tell AdWords to include query parameters to help identify campaigns, etc. with each pageview.

![](https://files.readme.io/24564f9-d3a7b64-Screen_Shot_2017-05-04_at_2.59.29_PM.png)

  3. Create your tracking template. This is creating a template for the urls to which AdWords directs people who click your add. Each campaign may have its own url that a clicked ad will lead to, and using this template will allow AdWords to automatically add other information to the query part of the URL.



The concept here is that you are going to tell AdWords that when creating the url for the link in one of your adds, put it through this template on a per-ad-view basis so that each click gets the right metadata or campaign information in the query.

So the template will look something like: `{base url you provided} ?(begin query)utm_campaign={campagin name}&utm_medium={campaign medium}` and will form a recipe for AdWords to add in the data. When creating the url, the parts in the `{}` braces (called Value Track Parameters, [more here](https://support.google.com/adwords/answer/2375447)) will be exchanged for actual values, while the parts outside will be included literally. A full list of available Value Track parameters is available [here](https://support.google.com/adwords/answer/6305348#urlinsertion).

Here is an actual functional example. You can start with copying this into the template in AdWords, and then you can adjust it as necessary after testing to meet your goals:
    
    
    {lpurl}?utm_campaign={campaignid}&utm_source=AdWords&utm_medium={network}&utm_content={creative}&utm_term={keyword}

Note that `{lpurl}` means the target url for the ad, which you provide to google when building a particular ad--so this will be replaced with the actual page on your site where you want people to go.

![](https://files.readme.io/116a534-7ea71d5-AdWords_URL_Auto_Tagging_Template_2.png)

  4. You can click "test" and it will run the template and create a test url that you can click to go to one of your pages--usually it will just use the homepage. If you click the test link and load your page, you should see the utm parameters in the url address bar, and you should also see a new pageview action in your profile in AIRIS, with campaign data associated with it right there in the interface. AdWords will also note any errors it detects, and you can use this to make sure you are getting all the right campaign data and utm parameters you want.

![](https://files.readme.io/7a5268d-bb3f2e8-AdWords_Auto_Tagging_URL_Template_Test.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Pro Tip:
> 
> You can create custom ValueTrack tags to carry things like human-readable campaign name. For more info on this, see [this AdWords Article](https://support.google.com/adwords/answer/6325879).

__Updated about 1 year ago

# AIQUA Triggers

## 

Overview

After [connecting your AIQUA account](doc:integration-aiqua#integration-guide), you can create AIRIS triggers to perform the following actions in a connected AIQUA account:

  * Send web push campaigns



* * *

## 

Send web push campaigns

### 

Prerequisites

Before creating a trigger to send a web push campaign, you'll need to complete the following:

  1. [Connect your AIQUA account](doc:integration-aiqua#integration-guide).
  2. Create an AIQUA web push campaign (either a regular campaign or trigger campaign).
  3. Retrieve the campaign ID of the web push campaign. The campaign ID can be found at the end of this campaign edit page's URL on the AIQUA dashboard, following the last `/`.
     1. For example, in the following URL, the campaign ID is `12345`: `https://aiqua.appier.com/regular_campaigns/edit/12345`

![](https://files.readme.io/5a966f3-aiqua-campaign-id.png)

### 

Creating a web push campaign trigger

  1. Go to **Automate > Triggers** and click **\+ New Trigger**.

![](https://files.readme.io/514d733-add-trigger.png)

  2. Set the following trigger settings:
     1. **Performed by** : Choose a user segment
     2. **Trigger when** : Choose the user event that triggers the target action
     3. **Frequency** : Choose how often the target actions should be executed

![](https://files.readme.io/aab9b11-trigger-settings.png)

  3. Under **Target Actions** , click **Add Target** , then select **Send Web Push** :

![](https://files.readme.io/144e6ec-trigger-add-target.png)

  4. Next, set the following AIQUA settings:
     1. **Connection** : Select the connected AIQUA account with the web push campaign.
     2. **Campaign ID** : Enter the campaign ID of the web push campaign you'd like to send.

![](https://files.readme.io/82af6ab-aiqua-trigger-settings.png)

  5. Under **Map Fields** , input the AIRIS field that should be mapped to the Appier ID. The Appier ID is used by AIQUA to uniquely identify users.

![](https://files.readme.io/bb1aacb-map-fields.png)

  6. Finally, click **Create** to complete the creation process.

![](https://files.readme.io/edbee09-create-trigger.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Data Onboarding

## 

Overview

There are several ways to collect the data required to build comprehensive user profiles and power capabilities such as segmentation, analytics, and triggers. Data can be sent to AIRIS using the following methods:

  * Appier SDK
  * Data loader
  * API



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Data retention
> 
> AIRIS supports a minimum data retention period of 2 years. To increase your organization's data retention period, contact your customer success manager.

### 

Appier SDK

Integrate the Appier SDK with your website or mobile app and use the SDK methods to collect default user and event data and log custom user and event data. Default data (generated properties) are automatically collected by the SDK, while custom data requires code modifications to your website or mobile app. For details, see [Tracking Overview](doc:tracking-overview).

### 

Data loader

Import data from a CSV file or third-party connection directly on the AIRIS console. With the data loader, you can create mappings between the external data source's columns and your AIRIS columns. For details, see [Data Loader Overview](doc:data-loader-overview).

### 

API

Import data directly from your server via API. For details, refer to the following API references:

  * [Offline Event API v2](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/reference/getting-starting-with-the-offline-event-api-v2)
  * [Bulk Upload Offline Users API](https://docs.aiqua.appier.com/reference/bulk-upload-offline-users)

# Correlation

Identify patterns in user behavior.

![](https://files.readme.io/0199abdfa738c5b65795e039ecbaf8846d8d88cf9321338eb409935d71671384-correlation-example.png)

Use correlation reports to identify patterns in user behavior, for example:

  * What types of products do customers of different age groups purchase the most?
  * What are the most-sold products, grouped by total revenue?
  * Which products are popular cross-sells?



In this guide, you'll learn how to create a correlation report, then read the resulting chart and table.

* * *

## 

Create a correlation report

To create a new correlation report, go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report > Correlation**. Next, configure the following report settings:

  1. Timeframe
  2. Performed by
  3. Classification
  4. Measurement



### 

Timeframe

Set a timeframe to determine which users to include in the report. If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included.

![](https://files.readme.io/590dc7a2c1978b39f61138df59511f13bad5f555ce788fd391583a06699aa180-correlation-settings-timeframe.png)

The timeframe's start and end date can be set in the following ways:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.



### 

Performed by

Select which segment of your audience the report should analyze. Either select an existing segment from the dropdown or click **+** to [create a new segment](doc:segments).

![](https://files.readme.io/97de9b6882f43d2dc67bae78e6c24ba5a5cf2126b4c77f35fa881153f7d67794-correlation-settings-performed-by.png)

### 

Classification

Determine how you'd like to group your audience.

![](https://files.readme.io/b87bbbec4a7f911101719ff3ed63a5daab786fdca47ce3e262a1f18665c7bd3e-correlation-settings-classification.png)

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Classify by**|  Select a classification event, event property, or attribute to group users by.  
**Buckets**|  Group data into buckets (data ranges) by specifying a list of values representing the maximum value (inclusive) for each bucket. Each bucket represents users with events, event properties, or attributes in the specified range, and is displayed on the report's X-axis. Each user can only be part of one bucket.  
  
  


> 🚧
> 
> Buckets are inclusive of the lower bound and exclusive of the upper bound.

If you select an classification event or event property, you can create buckets based on the following operators:

Operator| Description  
---|---  
**Count**|  Group by the number of times the classification event was completed.  
**Count Unique**|  Group by the number of unique times the classification event was completed.  
**Sum**|  Group by the classification event property's total sum.  
**Mean**|  Group by the classification event property's average value.  
**Min**|  Group by the classification event property's minimum value.  
**Max**|  Group by the classification event property's maximum value.  
**Last Touch**|  Group by the classification event property's most recent value.  
**First Touch**|  Group by the classification event property's oldest value.  
**Top**|  Group by the classification event property's most frequent value.  
  
Examples of classifications include:

  * User age. For example, groups of users 20-30, 30-40, and 40+.
  * Number of purchases from a product category. For example, groups of users with 0-5, 6-10, or 10+ purchases from the "Clothing" category.



#### 

Example: Find popular product categories for each age group

For example, to classify users by age, use the following classification settings.

Setting| Configuration| Description  
---|---|---  
**Classify by**| `age` (user attribute)| Users will be classified based on their age.  
**Buckets**| `20, 30, 40, 50, 60`| Users will be grouped as follows:  
• `age` < 20   
• 20 ≤ `age`< 30  
• 30 ≤ `age` < 40  
• 40 ≤ `age` < 50  
• 50 ≤ `age` < 60  
• 61 ≤`age `Each of these buckets will be displayed as a value on the X-axis of the report chart.  
  
### 

Measurement

Select a second attribute or event to measure in relation to the classification.

![](https://files.readme.io/9e5c4c9577c9fc457d864fcb5df88c6eccdd83beb8570e798e0d653de869dbb4-correlation-settings-measurement.png)

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Measure By**|  Select the second attribute or event and the operator to aggregate it with, such as **Count** or **Sum**.  
**Constraint**|  Add optional event property filters.  
**Dimension**|  Select an event property as a breakdown dimensions to further analyze each bucket.  
  
#### 

Example: Find which product categories are popular for each age group

Continuing with our example, to measure product purchases for each bucket (age range) of users, with an additional breakdown dimension for product category, set the following measurement settings.

Setting| Configuration| Description  
---|---|---  
**Measure by**|  • Event: `product_purchased`• Operator: `Count`| Measure the total number of purchases for each bucket (age group) of users.  
**Dimension**|  Event property: `product_category`| Add an additional breakdown dimension for the product category event property.  
  
* * *

## 

Reading the report chart

![](https://files.readme.io/0db8aa9f34c40828a63f7a974e71c23d61c54e4a60c9cb968fce761a2299e2c4-correlation-report-chart.gif)

Chart tab| Definition  
---|---  
**Count <selected_event>**| The count of measurement events/attributes completed by each classification group. The breakdown dimension is visible from this tab.  
  
Values on the X-axis are based on the classification groups. Counts shown on the X-axis represent the number of users in each group—not the event count.  
  
  
**Users**|  The total number of users in each classification group.  
**Matched users**|  The number of users in each classification group that met the measurement criteria.  
**Matched percent**|  The percentage of users in each classification group that met the measurement criteria (**Matched users** / **Users** x 100).  
  
### 

Chart example: Product purchases by age group

In our example, we'll analyze the most popular product category for users in the **20-30** age group (20 ≤ `age`< 30).

  * **Count`product_purchased`**
  * **Users**
  * **Matched users**
  * **Matched percent**



#### 

**Count`product_purchased`**

In the first tab, we can find the following details.

  * There were 37 total purchases for users in this group.
  * By hovering over the chart, we can see that "Eco-Friendly Kitchenware" is the most popular category for users in this group, with 9 purchases.



> 📘
> 
> Note that counts shown on the X-axis represent the number of users in each group—not the event count.

![](https://files.readme.io/3f7926dcf5250f926617c7df33bfcbaa7c59111865ee20561ba54d1628ecd504-correlation-report-chart-tab-1.png)

#### 

Users

From the second tab, we see that there are a total of 47 users in the **20-30** age group.

![](https://files.readme.io/7fe10e7597fcd243435925bb66e03fc4d1a9b711584b127c188f9123cc2e94ab-correlation-report-chart-tab-2.png)

#### 

Matched users

From the third tab, we see that 35 users in the **20-30** age group completed a purchase.

![](https://files.readme.io/b439196237b706dbb0ef74eb10a7cf253537ee1fc4083cc0c17afb0dcc406d27-correlation-report-chart-tab-3.png)

#### 

Matched percent

From the fourth tab, we see 74.47% (**Matched users** / **Users** x 100) of users in the **20-30** age group completed a purchase.

![](https://files.readme.io/453ceec91d77c3886c63200cbb52bdd26e181bf5e61d9b35d1fb5c89323fb2bc-correlation-report-chart-tab-4.png)

* * *

## 

Reading the report table

Below the chart is the report in table format detailing the event counts, user counts, and percentages for matched users and bucket percent, if you added a dimension.

If you selected a dimension, the **Bucket Percent** is displayed for each value, representing the proportion of users in a particular bucket corresponding to that dimension value.

![](https://files.readme.io/2f981d8824f68cf6ac6e301ae1462bae264dcf523d93e2d9988e5cf44da7ca98-correlation-report-table.png)

### 

Table example: Product purchases by age group

In our example, we'll analyze the most popular product category for the **20-30** group (20 ≤ `age`< 30).

![](https://files.readme.io/27a51d76f94567e149a4d4d1403c28a6ef4bd8b5c4de75528ec5504fe5b01570-correlation-report-table.png)

  1. There are a total of 37 purchases (**Count`product_purchased`**).
  2. 35 users completed a purchase (**Matched Users**), representing 74.47% of all users in this particular age group (**Matched Percent**).
  3. The most popular product category was "Eco-Friendly Kitchenware", with a total of 9 purchases.
     1. **Matched Users** : 9 users purchased from the "Eco-Friendly Kitchenware" category.
     2. **Matched Percent** : 19.15% of all users in this age group purchased from the "Eco-Friendly Kitchenware" category.
     3. **Bucket Percent** : 24.32% of purchases from users in the **20-30** age group purchased from the "Eco-Friendly Kitchenware" category.



* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Now that you've built your report, you can share it with your team. You can do this by clicking on the three vertical dots which will open an options menu for exporting, sharing, tagging, pinning, duplicating, and reloading your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/ee456a319c52e8afea4d9c3b3e0a5aebabc37a4021e5fcbe8a5fdcfda2c3f91f-report-share-export.png)

### 

Exporting

You can export reports to connected services, such as Google Sheets or Amazon S3, and download report in various formats, such as CSV, PDF, and HTML.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, for example, 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, 20 seconds.

### 

Snapshots

A snapshot is a view of the report at the time when the snapshot was created. Snapshots can be shared via public URL, allowing you to easily share reports with anyone—including external teams that aren't using AIRIS. The snapshot URL will link to a snapshot of your report at the time the snapshot was created.

To create a snapshot, click the three vertical dots and click **Snapshot**.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * Editing the report won't change previously-created snapshots.
>   * Snapshot URLs expire after seven days. The link will no longer be accessible after this period.
> 


### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to update the sharing settings for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu and select what team you want to share your report with.

__Updated 4 months ago

# Annotations

Use Annotations to tag important milestones and events to understand which initiatives are driving the most growth.

![](https://files.readme.io/a351443-Image_2021-04-22_at_2.27.45_PM.jpg)

Annotations allow you to tag a significant date to clearly see what impact it had on conversions, growth, and engagement. You create annotations for important events such as a new feature launch, the start of a campaign, the beginning and end of financial quarters, and much more!

Once you create an annotation, you'll be able to see it marked in [trend reports](doc:trends), and annotations can also be used in report **Timeframe** settings.

* * *

## 

How to add annotations

Go to **Configure > Annotations**. Click **\+ New Annotation** , then set the title, description, and event date.

![](https://files.readme.io/87b24f4-annotation-example.png)

You can optionally put the annotation in an annotation group. For example, you can create annotation groups like "Marketing Campaign" or "Feature Release".

To create a new group, click on the **Annotation Group** dropdown and select **Manage Annotation Groups**.

![](https://files.readme.io/a212cd2-create-annotation-group.png)

When you're satisfied with your settings, click **Create** to finish creating the annotation.

* * *

## 

Using annotations in reports

### 

Viewing annotations

In trend reports, you can show or hide annotations by clicking on the annotation icon.

![2386](https://files.readme.io/74e916a-Image_2021-04-22_at_2.42.11_PM.jpg)

When you show the annotations, you'll see them marked on the trend report graph.

Hover over the annotation dots to see more information.

![](https://files.readme.io/b28619e-woopra_annotations.png)

### 

Using annotations in the timeframe

You can also use annotations to set the **Timeframe** setting in reports. To do so, select the timeframe start or end point, then select to the **Annotations** tab and select an annotation. After selecting one, the timeframe setting will use the date from the annotation.

![](https://files.readme.io/ae9f8c6-Image_2021-04-22_at_2.47.18_PM.jpg)

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Archive

You can move unused schemas and annotations to an archive that can be restored later.

## 

How to Archive

Under any [Schema](doc:configuring-your-schema) or [Annotations](doc:annotations), click the ellipsis (3 dots), and click **Archive**.

![](https://files.readme.io/6caadef-Screen_Shot_2023-08-25_at_2.18.46_PM.png)

The archived schema or annotation will then be moved to the **Archive** page, which can be found under the **Configure > Manage** section in the menu or by clicking [here](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/archive).

![](https://files.readme.io/6dd5e92-Screen_Shot_2023-08-25_at_2.25.05_PM.png)

* * *

## 

Restoring archived files

Under the Archive section, select the schemas or annotations you'd like to restore, click the upper right dropdown, and select **Restore**.

![](https://files.readme.io/aa5fb21-Screen_Shot_2023-08-25_at_2.23.39_PM.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Attribution

Understanding what drives conversions is essential for optimizing traffic. Attribution reports offer a targeted view of the touchpoints and campaigns that contribute most to conversions, enabling you to measure the effectiveness of specific interactions and make data-driven decisions to enhance your engagement strategies.

  * Create an attribution report
  * Exporting and sharing
  * Attribution scenarios



* * *

## 

Create an attribution report

Go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report** > **Attribution** to create a new attribution report.

![](https://files.readme.io/35b6e5d7cfd65ad37dcafdadc783cfef50c868dc002f8bddf4f90f1f46e10025-report-create-attribution.png)

Next, configure report settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed by
  * Initial events
  * Touchpoints
  * Conversion events
  * Conversion tracking
  * Attribution window
  * Measure by
  * Attribution model



### 

Timeframe

If a user performs at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. Set the starting and ending dates as:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, for example, **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.

![](https://files.readme.io/448fe93-report-timeframe.png)

### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. Either select an existing segment or create a new segment by clicking the **+**. More info on how to create a segment can be found here: [Segments](doc:segments).

![](https://files.readme.io/a5b3333c1346e8ce834d8a0e3283cac062619a4e790638b66155414ebf49beaa-performed-by.png)

#### 

Performed by timeframe

The **Performed by** timeframe creates a subset of people captured by the **Timeframe** setting.

For example, if you want to run a marketing campaign to target users who are currently active and also made a purchase on last year's Black Friday, you would:

  1. Set **Timeframe** to the current month to select an initial group of all users that have performed at least one event in the current month.
  2. For the **Performed by** timeframe, select the purchase event with the date for last year's Black Friday.



Now, you'll have a list of all active users from the past month who purchased an item last Black Friday. The **Performed by** timeframe is usually less than or equal to the **Timeframe** range.

Here's a video explaining the difference between the two: [Timeframe vs Performed by timeframe](https://share.getcloudapp.com/E0uzzAvP).

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> AIRIS supports combining segmentation conditions using the **AND** and **OR** operators. To switch the operator, click on the operator name.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/0e4d3d2210bd611ba694d82fbcdbb1afbb4c87cafa6a261aafed31e9d308633f-Recording_2024-11-19_at_14.43.36.gif)

### 

Initial events

Select the first event (or starting point) where you want the attribution analysis to begin within the selected timeframe.

For example, if you run an email campaign that links users to your pricing page, you could use a page view on the pricing page as your initial event to analyze. You would select a `page_viewed` event where the `URL` contains `"pricing"`.

### 

Touchpoints

Specify an attribution event or multiple touchpoints to analyze which interactions guide users toward conversions. You can assign different weights to touchpoints by selecting an appropriate attribution model.

For example, if multiple campaigns brought users to your pricing page, you can analyze which campaigns drive the most conversions by tracking a `page_viewed` event where `campaign_name = exists`. Use [UTM tags](doc:campaign-tracking) to differentiate campaigns and gain deeper insights.

#### 

Touchpoints: Dimension

Configure breakdown dimensions for each touchpoint. If **Conversion tracking : All conversions** , you can select up to five dimensions. If **Conversion tracking: First conversion only** , you can only select one dimension.

For example, if you select the **Campaign name** and **URL** dimensions, the report will contain the following columns:

  * **Touch > Campaign name**
  * **Touch > URL**

![](https://files.readme.io/ac453be07b67333c90c275cc0d200ddc85ee484c48150759620f97d20f260137-touch-dimensions.png)

In the report chart, you can click on each segment of the pie chart to break down by dimension.

> 👍
> 
> You can also add further breakdown dimensions for conversion events.

![](https://files.readme.io/3d7c6deebecfdba61a383b3f5de91218660ed1f5737801ef5c5cb3456364fe49-attribution-chart-breakdown.gif)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Multiple touchpoints
> 
> If you add multiple touchpoints, dimensions are shared by all touchpoints. To edit dimensions, click on the first touchpoint.

### 

Conversion events

Defining the conversion event that you want to analyze, for example, submitting a form, making a payment, or registering for an account. You can select one or more conversion events.

#### 

Conversion events: Dimension

Configure breakdown dimensions for each conversion event. If **Conversion tracking: All conversions** , you can select up to five dimensions. If **Conversion tracking: First conversion only** , you can only select one dimension.

For example, if you select the **Hour of Visit** and **Day of Visit** dimensions, the report will contain the following columns:

  * **Conversion > Hour of Visit**
  * **Conversion > Day of Visit**

![](https://files.readme.io/e696fd7705614f23d7d7bc7dd3e86969793d01b3a55280e9f264d63d809d94a3-conversion-dimensions.png)   


> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Multiple conversion events
> 
> If you add multiple conversion events, dimensions are shared by all events. To edit dimensions, click on the first conversion event.

### 

Conversion tracking

This defines how many conversions would be included in the attribution report.

  * **All conversions** : Include all conversions from the initial event within the timeframe.
  * **First conversion only** : Consider the first conversion per profile within the timeframe and after the initial event.



### 

Attribution window

The **Attribution window** sets the timeframe for crediting touchpoints that lead to conversions, applicable only when **All conversions** are selected. You are allowed to set **Attribution window** to 30 days. For example, with a 2-day window, only interactions within two days of the conversion will be attributed.

### 

Measure by

If **Conversion tracking: All conversions** , you can add up to three metrics (counts or sums) to measure how each touchpoint influences actions or sales. If **Conversion tracking: First conversion only** , you can only select one metric.

  * If you select an event count, the report shows an attribution count based on your selected attribution model.
  * When you select a sum, it will display a dollar amount attributed to conversions.



Each metric has a dedicated tab in the report chart.

![](https://files.readme.io/18fca02df3f6eefaebabab2d9d8ce84dd3efec81d95a0f5322879a9294493e76-measure-by-chart-tabs.png)

In the report table, a new column is added for each metric.

![](https://files.readme.io/3bed98a336bf7f3e0bd1a0631983a6e377147bf7d4fd1d4c512a1bb0fd20eac1-measure-by-table.png)

### 

Attribution model

Determines how you want to assign credit for the conversion. For example, if a user visited your site from a Google ad and later came back from a Facebook ad before finally converting, the attribution model determines which ad gets credit. The model can be used to tell AIRIS how you want to credit the conversion.

First touch, last touch, and linear touch are the most common, but we also support more granular models such as time decay and multi-touch.

Attribution model| Description| Example  
---|---|---  
**First touch**|  The first touch receives 100% of the credit for the conversion.| Google would receive credit for the conversion.  
**Last touch**|  The last touch receives 100% of the credit for the conversion.| Facebook would be credited with the conversion.  
**Time decay**|  Touches closer to the conversion in time receive the most credit. Enter the decay rate in seconds, minutes, hours, days, weeks, or months.| If you chose a decay rate of 2 hours, the touchpoints within these 2 hours will receive more credit than events that happen outside this time window.  
**Linear**|  Credit is distributed evenly across all touches.| Google and Facebook would each get 50-50 credit for the conversion.  
**Multi-touch**|  Define the percentage of credit that the first and last touches will receive. The remaining credit is distributed evenly across the remaining touches.| Credit for the conversion is split evenly between Google and Facebook.  
  
* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Once your report is built, you can share it with your team. Click the three vertical dots to open the options menu, where you can manage your report. For example, you can export, sync, take a snapshot, share, tag, pin to the dashboard, duplicate, or delete your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/79566421ebc30fafe348d93c416cc331abb14a71719028c82df85dbefcb84c3b-vertical-dots.png)

### 

Downloading

You can download the report in various formats, such as CSV, JSON-lines, PDF, and HTML. Click **Export** and select **Download report**.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, for example, 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, or 20 seconds.

![](https://files.readme.io/74889a9139f926f93a243ca07b8e71f1ee718f9a1340a3cce0c841664487fba6-Download-report.png)

### 

Snapshots

A snapshot captures the report as it was when the snapshot was taken. Snapshots can be shared via public URL, allowing you to easily share reports with anyone—including external teams that aren't using AIRIS. The snapshot URL will link to a snapshot of your report at the time the snapshot was created.

To create a snapshot, click the three vertical dots and click **Snapshot**.

![](https://files.readme.io/3b37f93934a1fdf23336348f51c201f241efd5767287d76346c88d266efcce70-snapshot-report.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * A snapshot is a view of the report at the time when the snapshot was created. Editing the report will not change previously created snapshots.
>   * Snapshot URLs expire after seven days. The link will no longer be accessible after this period.
> 


### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to update the sharing settings for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu of three vertical dots and select what team you want to share your report with.

![](https://files.readme.io/7c9c229f4fd16b8a6fdda6548629d7ce0285d568f4ffda26f10ba1496f4fca11-share-attribution-report.png)

* * *

## 

Attribution scenarios

The following scenarios demonstrate how conversion and attribution are calculated.

  * First touch model: Standard conversion
  * First touch model: No conversion counted
  * Last touch model: Any initial condition
  * Linear model: Multiple touchpoints
  * Time decay model



### 

First touch model: Standard conversion

In this scenario, the user completes multiple events before completing a checkout.

Setting| Details  
---|---  
**Timeframe**|  30 days ago  
**Initial events**|  Email opened  
**Touchpoint**|  Product viewed  
**Conversion**|  Checkout completed  
**Conversion tracking**|  All conversions  
**Attribution window**|  Seven days ago  
**Attribution model**|  First touch  
  
Using the **First touch** attribution model, the product viewed event for Product A receives full credit for the conversion.

![](https://files.readme.io/4eba10d871214e30124daeaf8c451f827d9b1f375aeda00b34580a4b6ef8671f-attribution-first-touch-standard.png)

### 

First touch model: No conversion counted

In this scenario, the user completes a checkout after opening an email and viewing a product. However:

  * The user opened the email outside the configured attribution timeframe.
  * The user viewed the product outside the attribution window.



Setting| Details  
---|---  
**Timeframe**|  Seven days ago  
**Initial events**|  Email opened  
**Touchpoint**|  Product viewed  
**Conversion**|  Checkout completed  
**Conversion tracking**|  All conversions  
**Attribution window**|  Two days ago  
**Attribution model**|  First touch  
  
As a result, no credit is attributed to the events, and the conversion is not counted within the attribution model.

![](https://files.readme.io/fb57d68c96c132604af7b4c521d74acc18e5c1e8814ec23f0a2b8bb7f732c13f-no-conversion.png)

### 

Last touch model: Any initial condition

In this scenario, the user completes any initial event, views a product, and completes a checkout.

Setting| Details  
---|---  
**Timeframe**|  30 days ago  
**Initial events**|  Any event  
**Touchpoint**|  Product viewed  
**Conversion**|  Checkout completed  
**Conversion tracking**|  All conversions  
**Attribution window**|  Seven days  
**Attribution model**|  Last touch  
  
Using the **Last touch** attribution model, the product viewed event just before the checkout is fully credited for driving the conversion.

![](https://files.readme.io/08185e3c13ab2bd236c6d38818b33866ccf69ec78955bd9f126d15cc9e7ede78-attribution-last-touchpoint.png)

### 

Linear model: Multiple touchpoints

In this scenario, the user opens multiple emails, views multiple products, and completes two separate checkouts.

Setting| Details  
---|---  
**Timeframe**|  30 days ago  
**Initial events**|  Email opened  
**Touchpoint**|  Product viewed  
**Conversion**|  Checkout completed  
**Conversion tracking**|  All conversions  
**Attribution window**|  Seven days  
**Attribution model**|  Linear  
  
Using the **Linear** attribution model:

  * Conversion 1 attribution: Credit is distributed equally across both product viewed events (Product A and Product B) before the first checkout completed event.
  * Conversion 2 attribution: The second product viewed event receives full credit as it was the only touchpoint before the second checkout completed event.

![](https://files.readme.io/843df7d317236af32d0b0336d98978b9fb2308c9326c57e5c8e9db18965fc8a0-attribution-multiple-touchpoint.png)

### 

Time decay model

In this scenario, the user opens an email, interacts with multiple product viewed touchpoints, and completes two checkout events.

Setting| Details  
---|---  
**Timeframe**|  30 days ago  
**Initial events**|  Email opened  
**Touchpoint**|  Product viewed  
**Conversion**|  Checkout completed  
**Conversion tracking**|  All conversions  
**Attribution window**|  10 days  
**Attribution model**|  Time decay (Decay rate: 7 days)  
  
Using the **Time decay** attribution model, credit is weighted more heavily toward touchpoints that occur closer to the conversion. When using this model, you must select a decay window (for example, 7 days) to determine how touchpoints are attributed:

  * Touchpoints within decay window receive the most credit.
  * Touchpoints outside the decay window but still within the attribution window receive significantly less credit.
  * Touchpoints outside the attribution window receive no credit.



The diagram below shows both attribution windows overlaid in a single timeline, illustrating how each conversion is independently evaluated with its own attribution calculations.

![](https://files.readme.io/bfcf11e4998dc4e7cd4c9c433c3dc3f859363c17275884010068d4e690e7f224-attribution-multiple-touchpoint-decay-model-2-layers.png)

  * Conversion 1 attribution: Credit is distributed across product viewed events before the first checkout.
    * The touchpoint where Product D was viewed receives the highest credit (96.52%) due to its proximity to the conversion.
    * The touchpoint where Product C was viewed receives a smaller portion (3.47%) as it occurs earlier.
    * The touchpoint where Product B was viewed is within the attribution window but outside the decay window. This touchpoint gets less credit (0.01%) than touchpoints that are closer to the conversion and within the decay window.
    * The touchpoint where Product A was viewed occurs outside the attribution window and receive no credit.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> If only one touchpoint falls within the attribution window, it receives 100% of the credit, even if it’s outside the decay window. Credit is always distributed among eligible touchpoints.

![](https://files.readme.io/f01af5f10116bf4b57d0a719c5eb766fb65df16d9267bd4cc5e10f3cf10ba2c1-attribution-multiple-touchpoint-decay-model-differ.png)

  * Conversion 2 attribution: Only the touchpoint where Product D was viewed falls within the decay window before the second checkout and receives full credit. The touchpoints where Products A, B, and C were viewed fall outside the attribution window and receive no credit.

![](https://files.readme.io/2a4886ddfd2b53371ac3e831c90217e405c725af7c40bd82567821c8f4b1f192-attribution-multiple-touchpoint-decay-model-full.png)

__Updated 3 months ago

# Botbonnie Triggers

https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/botbonnie-triggers

## Introduction

  * [Introduction to AIRIS](/docs/introduction)
  * [What is AIRIS: Product Capabilities __](/docs/what-is-airis-product-capabilities)
    * [Understanding Data in AIRIS __](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/types-of-tracked-data)
    * [Challenges the AIRIS Platform Solves](/docs/challenges-the-woopra-platform-solves)



## Getting Started

  * [Getting Started with AIRIS __](/docs/getting-started-guide-the-woopra-essentials-mix)

# BotBonnie Triggers

## 

Overview

After [connecting your BotBonnie account](doc:integration-botbonnie#integration-guide), you can create AIRIS triggers to perform the following actions in a connected BotBonnie account:

  * Start a WebChat bot



* * *

## 

Start a WebChat bot

### 

Prerequisites

Before creating a trigger to start a WebChat bot, make sure you've finished setting up the WebChat bot in your connected BotBonnie account.

### 

Creating a trigger to start a web chat

  1. Go to **Automate > Triggers** and click **\+ New Trigger**.

![](https://files.readme.io/514d733-add-trigger.png)

  2. Set the following trigger settings:
     1. **Performed by** : Select a user segment.
     2. **Trigger when** : Select the user event that triggers the target action.
     3. **Frequency** : Determine how often the target actions should be executed.

![](https://files.readme.io/aab9b11-trigger-settings.png)

  3. Under **Target Actions** , click **Add Target** and select **Start WebChat Bot** :

![](https://files.readme.io/72a3ea8-bb-trigger-add-target.png)

  4. Configure the following BotBonnie settings for the action you just added:
     1. **Connection** : Select an existing BotBonnie connection from the dropdown menu or create a new connection.
     2. **BotBonnie Page** : Select the WebChat bot you'd like to use.
     3. **AIRIS field** : Select the AIRIS field which corresponds to the BotBonnie identifier.
     4. **Start conversation module** : Select the flow module which will be used to start the conversation.

![](https://files.readme.io/071b7bc-bb-webchat-target-action-settings.png)

  4. Finally, click **Create** to finish creating the trigger.

![](https://files.readme.io/eab5acf-create-webchat-bot-trigger.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# System Properties

## 

Overview

The AIRIS tracking system is designed to be completely customizable, where we track whatever you send us. You can define what is sent after the fact using [Schema](doc:configuring-your-schema).

However, the AIRIS tracking system does some things automatically when it comes to your data. When sending a tracking event for instance, even if you don't explicitly include the user's IP address in the custom event properties, the AIRIS system will record the IP from the HTTP request itself. Then, in the case of IP addresses, more data enrichment may occur, such as adding location data (region, etc.), company data (owner of the IP address), and more.

This section explains all of the automatic data recording and enrichment that happens in AIRIS in all three of the data scopes: [user data, visit data, and event data.](doc:generated-visit-properties)

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Calendly Tracking Guide

## 

Overview

AIRIS does not have a direct integration with Calendly. However, you can use the following code with Zapier to track Calendly events in AIRIS.

To track Calendly events, we need to add additional code to the page that contains the Calendly iframe code on your site. This code will send a webhook to [Zapier](doc:zapier) that contains a Calendly inviteeUrl and eventUrl that we will use to retrieve the user's email. This is done by first catching the hook in Zapier, which contains the necessary data that we'll then use to send a GET request to Calenedly to retrieve the email and event details. We'll finally capture the GET request response and then send that data as a track event to AIRIS.

* * *

## 

Step 1: Create a Zap

In Zapier, create a new Zap with a Catch Hook.

![](https://files.readme.io/4d51cc0-Image_2023-05-30_at_7.43.54_PM.jpg)

Under **Test** , there will be a webhook URL. Copy this URL and paste it into the following code in step 2.

* * *

## 

Step 2: Add the Code

The following code needs to be added to the page that contains the Calendly iframe code on your site.
    
    
    function calendlyHandler(e) {
      if (e.data.event && e.data.event.indexOf('calendly') !== 0) {
        return;
      }
    
      switch (e.data.event) {
    
        /* Start Zapier Augmentation */
        case 'calendly.event_scheduled':
          fetch('Replace this with Zappier Webhook URL', {
            method: 'POST',
            body: JSON.stringify({
              cookie: window.woopra.cookie,
              eventUrl: e.data.payload.event.uri,
              inviteeUrl: e.data.payload.invitee.uri
            })
          });
        /* End Zapier Augmentation */
    
        default:
          woopra.track(e.data.event, e.data.payload);
          break;
      }
    }
    
    window.addEventListener('message', calendlyHandler);

* * *

## 

Step 3: Schedule a Test Meeting

Once you have added the code on your site, schedule a test meeting so a webhook gets sent to the catch hook in Zapier.

Go to Zapier, click the **Test** section in the Catch Hook, and select the webhook that was sent from the Calendly test. You may need to click the dropdown of the request and click **Load more** to select the most recent test.

![](https://files.readme.io/b8ab360-Image_2023-05-30_at_7.56.44_PM.jpg)

When you receive the event, you'll have the necessary data to configure the rest of the webhooks.

* * *

## 

Step 4: Send a GET Request to Calendly

### 

Add an Action in Zapier

Next, add a new action, "Webhook", and select the event as a GET request.

Under the **URL** field, you'll now have the Invitee URL from the previous Catch Hook that you can add. The rest of the settings should be the following:

![](https://files.readme.io/66942fa-Screenshot_2023-05-09_at_4.30.40_PM.png)

### 

Create API Token in Calendly

Follow this link to create an [API Token](https://calendly.com/integrations/api_webhooks). Select **Personal access token** and name it. Once you have the token, copy it and enter it under the **Header** section, as shown above. You'll need to add the text "Authorization" in the first box and "Bearer" followed by the copied token in the second box. Click **Continue**.

* * *

## 

Step 5: Add Woopra Track Action

Finally, add another action and search for "Woopra" (use the latest version). Select a track action and enter your project name.

Next, configure the action as follows:

![](https://files.readme.io/3852886-Image_2023-05-30_at_8.14.00_PM.jpg)

Save the Zap and you're done!

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

# Campaign and Referrer Data: What's the difference?

Understand the difference between campaign and referrer data, and when to use them.

There are different ways to analyze where a user came from and how they landed on your site. In this document, we'll break down some scenarios and explain when to use certain AIRIS system properties such as **campaign source** or **referrer type**.

## 

What is referrer data?

Wikipedia states:  
"_In HTTP, 'referrer' is the name of an optional HTTP header field that identifies the address of the web page (i.e., the URI or IRI), which is linked to the resource being requested. By checking the referrer, the server providing the new web page can see where the request originated._

_In the most common situation, this means that when a user clicks a hyperlink in a web browser, causing the browser to send a request to the server holding the destination web page, the request may include the Referer field, which indicates the last page the user was on (the one where they clicked the link)._ "

AIRIS will attempt to pull this referrer data that's stored in the user's browser (if available) and we'll use that referrer URI to determine the AIRIS fields **Referrer URL** and [**Referrer type**](doc:generated-visit-properties#referrer-types). There is logic on our backend that determines the referrer type based on rules such as: if the referrer URI contains "google.com" then the referrer type is "Search."

You can read more on [Why Referrer Tracking Can Be Inaccurate](doc:why-referer-tracking-can-be-inaccurate), but the main point is that tracking applications are not always able to pull referrer data from the user's browser. Typically, this field should be used to get a general sense of where users are coming from and not a definitive answer.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> What is the difference between URL and URI in AIRIS?
> 
> URL and URI can differ depending on the platform you're using. However, in AIRIS, we define URL as anything after the domain name. For example, if you visit the page `<https://www.appier.com/blog>` the URL field in AIRIS would be `/blog`. The URI in AIRIS is the complete address including the `https`.

## 

What is campaign data?

The **Campaign Properties** in AIRIS are based on UTM data extracted from the URIs of pageviews on your site. You can use a [URL builder tool](https://ga-dev-tools.google/campaign-url-builder/) to add UTM tags to external links that direct to your website.

We recommend using UTM tags in all external links to your website (paid or not) when possible. This way, when a user lands on your page containing UTM tags, they'll land on a page that looks like: "[http://www.yourwebsite.com/?utm_campaign=promo&utm_source=facebook&utm_medium=web_paid_ppc&utm_content=banner](http://www.yourwebsite.com/?utm_campaign=promo&utm_source=facebook&utm_medium=web_paid_ppc&utm_content=banner)"

Campaign data will **always** be more accurate because it does not rely on referrer data (which may or may not exist) and the URI is always recorded on pageview events in AIRIS. When a URI is recorded on a pageview event, AIRIS will automatically extract the UTM data into separate fields under Campaign Properties.

![1299](https://files.readme.io/5fa918f-Image_2021-12-01_at_10.24.42_AM.jpg)

All of these "campaign" fields are extracted from utm_campaign, utm_source, etc.

## 

When should I use campaign data or referrer data?

Not all external links contain UTM data. For example, UTM data is not available for organic traffic from search engines.

Usually, paid campaigns will contain UTM tags, so AIRIS's campaign-related fields should be used to analyze paid campaign performance. Since there are several UTM tags you can define such as `utm_medium`, you can specify whether the link was paid or not (e.g. `utm_medium=web_paid_ppc`). This makes it easy to analyze specific campaigns and differentiate between paid and non-paid traffic.

Referrer fields should be used to analyze general traffic trends if UTM data isn't available. Since many new browsers might block referrer data, you'll often see a lot of "direct" [referrer type](doc:generated-visit-properties#referrer-types) traffic. This can mean there was no referrer data for the pageview visit.

> 👍
> 
> You can also create [Custom Event Schema Properties](doc:custom-schema-properties) that can combine campaign data and referrer data using [Formulas](doc:operations). For example, we could create a formula that says, if campaign data exists for a recorded pageview event, use 'campaign source' and if not, use the 'referrer type'.

## 

Breakdown of AIRIS Campaign and Referrer fields

AIRIS Field| Description| Example  
---|---|---  
Referrer Type| Uses the most recent referrer for an event. Uses the referrer URL to determine the ["type"](doc:generated-visit-properties#referrer-types).| direct, internal, search, backlink, social, email, PPC  
First Referrer Type| Saves the first referrer type recorded for the user.| direct, internal, search, backlink, social, email, PPC  
Referrer URL| Uses the most recent referrer URL for an event if available.| <https://www.somepage.com>  
Campaign Name| Extracts `utm_campaign=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. The name of the campaign.| campaign xyz  
Campaign Source| Extracts `utm_source=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. Typically where the ad was displayed.| google, facebook, instagram, etc.  
Campaign Medium| Extracts `utm_medium=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. Typically the type of ad.| web, paid, email, tweet, etc.  
Campaign Content| Extracts `utm_content=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. Typically used if running similar ads.| topbanner, rightsidebanner, specific_location, temporarysale, etc.  
Campaign Term| Extracts `utm_term=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. Typically associated with Google Adwords or search/keyword terms.| running+shoes, journey+analytics, coffee+cups, etc.  
Campaign ID| Extracts `utm_id=` from the pageview URI that AIRIS records. Some campaigns might have an ID.| id_xyz  
First Campaign Name, Source, Medium, etc.| Saves the first recorded visit that contained UTM tags. Note that the first visit from a user might not have UTM tags, but this will still record the first instance if there is one later on.| See examples above  
  
> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Notes
> 
> Campaign data can also be associated with other events other than pageview events such as some email events or other custom events. This will depend on how you're sending these events to AIRIS.

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Challenges the AIRIS Platform Solves

## 

1) Non-linear Customer Journeys and Challenges in Data Democratization

The modern customer journey is highly complex and non-linear in nature. To understand the customer experience across channels, teams and tools, you must expand beyond the silos of strictly marketing or product-related touchpoints.

Today’s businesses need to derive insights from across the organization, leveraging marketing, paid and unpaid advertising, customer success, product, support, sales and other groups within the company.

Most businesses find it challenging to democratize data across the organization and obtain a 360-degree view of their customers to drive end-to-end business objectives.

## 

2) Limited Understanding of Product Engagement

It’s crucial to know how prospective users engage with your product or service in order to identify leads at the right time and on the right channel. This is especially true for SaaS companies that offer a ‘freemium’ [model](https://www.business2community.com/product-management/build-sales-accelerating-product-qualified-lead-engine-01696527) when acquiring new customers. Often, they spend an unreasonable amount of time and resources on lead acquisition by sending repeated communications to prospects on an action such as a free trial sign up form.

Unfortunately, given the vast array of options available out there, such actions are no more telling of the lead’s propensity to purchase. What’s worse is that unwanted emails or calls can do more harm than good by deterring them from your product or service altogether.

For a company focused on the customer experience, actions such as repeated use of certain features, downloading how-to tutorials, and viewing software setup videos are much more indicative of interested prospects. However, there are few tools that track behavior across a product or service while enabling teams to engage with their users in real-time.

## 

3) Functional Data Siloes

With an estimated 66 different SaaS applications that are in use per enterprise – primarily for web and social traffic based analytics activity – companies are unable to properly leverage the data flowing in their organization to generate meaningful and timely insights.

Moreover, the market is crowded with solutions promising results such as higher conversion rates that are generating further confusion about what solution to implement, at what cost, and at what time. Such a conundrum gives rise to data siloes that often fail to render timely insights to business users. Most importantly, companies often fail to realize how difficult it is to bring all the customer-centric data together in order to find a single source of truth.

Given the fragmentation of investments in tools across all sizes of enterprises, a unifying customer journey [analytics solution](https://blog.woopra.com/choosing-the-right-data-integration-tool-for-your-business-4ae46e62551b) integrates with all the relevant applications to provide a singular view of each customer and better analyze their interaction and needs across all channels. Companies need to anticipate their customers’ behaviors and shift how they think about and engage throughout the decision journey.

## 

4) Inability to Monetize from Data

Although every organization is now aware that data is their gold mine, few have managed to establish a sound data strategy that will allow them to consistently monetize from it. Most companies have trouble simplifying their data architectures to build a single layer that augments all the relevant customer-related information in one central location. Connecting with leads and channelizing efforts to truly convert engagement to revenue is that piece of the puzzle that unfortunately many are still trying to solve.

# Clearbit

Pointers for setting up and using the Clearbit AppConnect Integrations

Clearbit is a data enrichment partner. Clearbit can enrich your profiles based on email, and IP address. The Clearbit functionality is separated into two apps. Basic email address-based enrichment is available to all AIRIS and Clearbit customers, while the ip-address-based enrichment from Clearbit's Reveal product, is a separate premium integration, and is only available to enterprise AIRIS users.

# 

Example Use Cases

## 

Clearbit Reveal

Clearbit Reveal is quite simply, a game changer. The prototypical use case is more or less the following: A brand new user comes to your website. You send their IP address to Clearbit Reveal. Clearbit responds with company information. You score the company as a lead, and determine a specific personalized experience, or even personalized chat pop-up to show the brand new user to your site. For example, a Drift Message saying: "Hello! I see you work at AIRIS! Because you are a B2B SAAS company, I think My Product would be a great fit for you. How about a personal product tour?" Similarly, you could trigger Optimizely to show a given experiment, or tell Zendesk to prioritize all support and information request tickets from this user.

### 

Send personalized messages to anonymous site visitors with AIRIS, Drift, and Reveal

Here are the steps to create the example trigger from above, using Clearbit data to inform a Personalized Drift live chat message to new anonymous users to your marketing site.

  1. Pre-requisites:


  * Make sure Drift and Clearbit Reveal are both installed and authorized in AIRIS.
  * Make sure Drift is properly installed on your website. See their docs for help.


  2. Create your trigger: In AIRIS, go to Automations > Triggers and create a new trigger.
  3. Define Your trigger:


  * Define the user segment (the criteria a person must meet to cause this trigger to fire.) In this case, the user segment will be something like: `"People who DID: "clearbit.identified_by_ip" AND who ARE: "clearbit_company.name exists, AND clearbit_company.customer_model is 'B2B' AND clearbit_company.model is 'SAAS' "`
  * Determine when this trigger should fire for people who match the above criteria. We want this to fire on a pageview event, because on your website is where using a tool like Drift makes sense. (We couldn't fire a drift message based on, say, and "email received" event, can we?) So we will set up the trigger to `"Fire When: 'pageview' "`
  * Determine the frequency: We probably don't want this to fire constantly on every pageview event, because that would be annoying. Let's say "once per visit" so that once they dismiss the chat bubble or leave the page, they will not be shown the same chat again immediately, but rather on their next session (the next time they visit our site.)
  * Click "save" to make sure all these settings are saved


  4. Set up your Trigger Action:


  * Find the Drift Trigger Action called **Show the Welcome or Away Message** in the menu.
  * Customize your message in the trigger actions settings. You can use AIRIS templates in the message content as well. In this case, our message will look like:  
`"Hello! I see you work at ${visitor.clearbit_company.name}. Because you are a B2B SAAS company, I think My Product would be a great fit for you. How about a personal product tour?"`
  * Click "save" again to save the trigger action settings.

# Cohort

![](https://files.readme.io/986a8de-1_pKxr-qmrQMQQm4x5YQmLYQ.png)

## 

Overview

Cohort analysis allows you to measure the performance of cohorts, those who share common behaviors, over time. By breaking up your users into cohorts, you can understand how different activities across the organization impact specific cohorts throughout their lifecycle.

Teams across the organization can use AIRIS's Cohort analytics to measure product engagement, marketing campaign efficiency, revenue trends, sales retention, and more to increase customer health and stickiness over time.

## 

Cohort report structure

A Cohort analysis report is structured in two parts:

  1. The top chart aggregates all of the cohorts together, showing you the overall (average) retention rate of your users within a range of dates.
  2. The lower table displays the retention of individual cohorts independently to monitor any changes in retention rates over time within your date range.



* * *

## 

Creating a cohort report

To create a new cohort report go to **Analyze** , then click **\+ New Report** > **Cohorts**.

![](https://files.readme.io/7dabe55-cohort-add-report.png)

After creating the report, you can configure the following settings:

  * Timeframe
  * Performed By
  * Cohort
  * Repeat Events
  * Time Unit



### 

Timeframe

If a user performed at least one event in the specified timeframe, their data will be included in this report. The starting and ending dates for the timeframe can be set in the following ways:

  * **Relative** : Select a time that is relative to the present day, such as **90 days ago** or **Today**. This timeframe is dynamic and updates in real time.
  * **Absolute** : Select a specific date, such as **January 1st, 2023**.
  * **Annotations** : Use the [annotation date](doc:annotations#using-annotations-in-the-timeframe) from an existing annotation.

![](https://files.readme.io/448fe93-report-timeframe.png)

### 

Performed by

Determines the segment or group of people you'll be analyzing. Either select an existing segment or create a new segment by clicking the **+**. More info on how to create a Segment can be found here: [Segments](doc:segments).

#### 

Performed by timeframe

The **Performed by** timeframe creates a subset of people captured by the **Timeframe** setting.

For example, if you want to run a marketing campaign to target users who are currently active and also made a purchase on last year's Black Friday, you would:

  1. Set **Timeframe** to the current month to select an initial group of all users that have performed at least one event in the current month.
  2. For the **Performed by** timeframe, select the event “purchased item” with the date for last year's Black Friday.



Now, you'll have a list of all active users from the past month who purchased an item last Black Friday. Typically, the **Performed by** timeframe is less than or equal to the **Timeframe** range.

Here's a video explaining the difference between the two: [Timeframe vs Performed by timeframe](https://share.getcloudapp.com/E0uzzAvP).

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> AIRIS supports combining segmentation conditions using the **AND** and **OR** operators. To switch the operator, click on the operator name.
> 
> ![](https://files.readme.io/72170ae-report-operator-selection.gif)

### 

Cohort

Select the event to track for the cohort.

### 

Repeat Events

Repeat events are subsequent events demonstrating user engagement with your product, e.g. a customer starting a meeting, creating a report, sending an email, or returning to your website.

### 

Metric

The aggregate count or sum of the event you are measuring. The available options can change depending on your **Repeat Events** selection.

For example, you can analyze the report based on the number of users, visits, or events. If, for the **Repeat Events** , you select events that contain an amount or duration property, you'll also be able to select metrics, like sums of properties with numeric types, e.g. sum of total or sum of duration.

![](https://files.readme.io/0aa65a9-Image_2023-03-29_at_3.45.18_PM.jpg)

### 

Time Unit

The time unit is the amount of time to track after the user joins the cohort, e.g. 5 days, 2 weeks, 10 months.

### 

Others

You can click the checkbox to disregard the initial timeframe to initial events and include all people who performed the repeat event for all time.

* * *

## 

Exporting and sharing

Now that you've built your report, you can share it with your team. You can do this by clicking on the three vertical dots which will open an option menu for exporting, sharing, tagging, pinning, duplicating and reloading your report.

![](https://files.readme.io/ee456a319c52e8afea4d9c3b3e0a5aebabc37a4021e5fcbe8a5fdcfda2c3f91f-report-share-export.png)

### 

Exporting

You can export reports to connected services, such as Google Sheets or Amazon S3, and download report in various formats, such as CSV, PDF, and HTML.

Note that **CSV (no formatting)** will download some columns, such as `average_session_length`, in total seconds. Exporting the regular **CSV** for these columns will download timestamps in a string format, e.g. 3 minutes, 21 seconds - 8 minutes, 20 seconds.

### 

Snapshots

A snapshot is a view of the report at the time when the snapshot was created. Snapshots can be shared via public URL, allowing you to easily share reports with anyone—including external teams that aren't using AIRIS. The snapshot URL will link to a snapshot of your report at the time the snapshot was created.

To create a snapshot, click the three vertical dots and click **Snapshot**.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
>   * Editing the report won't change previously-created snapshots.
>   * Snapshot URLs expire after seven days. The link will no longer be accessible after this period.
> 


### 

Sharing

Reports are not shared with your team by default, so you'll need to update the sharing settings for others to access it. Click **Share** from the menu and select what team you want to share your report with.

__Updated 11 months ago

* * *

# Credit Usage

View and manage your project’s credit usage.

Credits are the basic unit for measuring your account's usage, providing full transparency into your project's overall usage and costs. The following operations consume credits:

  * Scheduled batches
  * Report email subscriptions
  * Segment exports
  * Triggers



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The exact number of credits consumed by each operation is determined by the volume of data processed and the operation's complexity.

You can view your project's credit usage at any time to monitor usage. For tips and recommendations on optimizing credit usage, see Managing credit usage.

* * *

## 

View your project's credit usage

To view a log of the credit usage in your project, go to **Configure** , then go to **Admin > Credit Usage**.

![](https://files.readme.io/2f7839c8161ae86166d21ccdaf441518de418835c1248f0a5a557a54b0541f8a-credit-usage-nav.png)

* * *

## 

Managing credit usage

Follow the tips below to optimize your credit usage and avoid exceeding your expected quota usage.

  * Automations
  * Predictions
  * Reports



### 

Automations

Avoid credit usage due to unnecessary automations by:

  * Scheduling recurring jobs at the slowest frequency that matches business needs.
  * Reviewing and toggling off unnecessary automations.



### 

Reports

  * Use filters and smaller timeframes.
  * Minimize unnecessary formulas or complex calculated metrics.
  * Preview or sample relative small amount of data before full execution.



### 

Predictions

The basic package allows prediction usage for up to 25,000 concurrent users per day. Monitor your real prediction usage to determine if you actually need to raise the daily cap.

You can also temporarily raise caps as needed, for example, during campaigns, then lower them afterwards. To change your daily prediction cap, contact Appier Support ([[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#284d5b5b775b5d5858475a5c68495858414d5a064b4745)).
