# Schema

## 

Overview

Schemas are the skeleton of an AIRIS project. Schemas are declarations of event and user property metadata, defining how tracked data should be read and processed. The schema configuration tells AIRIS:

  * What type of data it is
  * How the data should be displayed
  * How the data can be aggregated



In addition, note that:

  * Editing a schema doesn't affect the actual tracking of the data, it only changes how AIRIS reads and displays tracked data.
  * Deleting a schema won't delete any existing data that has already been collected.



> 🚧
> 
> ### 
> 
> Important
> 
> Please exercise caution when updating the **Key** value in a schema. Doing so can result in duplicate events in your schemas.

### 

Schema auto-generation

Whenever AIRIS receives a new event or user property without an existing schema, a basic schema is automatically created so that the event or user property will be visible in reports and searches. For a schema to be automatically generated for an event or user property:

  * The data must only contain letters, numbers, underscores, dashes, and spaces.
  * You must not have more than 500 existing schemas. For example, if your account has 500 user property schemas and 0 event schemas, AIRIS won't automatically generate a schema for new property data, but will continue to automatically generate schemas for event data.



You can edit the schema to customize the schemas and modify fields that can't be auto-generated, such as the display name or template. If the schema already exists, AIRIS will not modify it when you start sending other properties. This means:

  * New properties won't be automatically added to the existing schema.
  * To update the schema, you'll need to either delete it and wait for the event to be tracked again, or manually make the property modifications yourself.



* * *

## 

Editing a schema

Basic schemas are automatically created from custom events and user properties that are tracked, or from events generated by integrated apps. After the basic schema is generated, you should customize the configuration to suit your needs.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> To edit a schema, you must be an Admin user.

Navigate to the **Configure** section, go to **Manage** , and select **Event Schema** or **User Schema**.

![](https://files.readme.io/55e8cc8-Screen_Shot_2024-02-29_at_10.47.16_AM.png)

There are two types of schemas in AIRIS: user schemas and event schemas. Refer to the following section, Schema types for details.

* * *

## 

Schema types

  * User schema
  * Event schema



### 

User schema

The user schema defines the custom user properties that are being tracked. The name, email, and company name are added to the user schema by default, but AIRIS will also add a schema for custom user data that is tracked.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note: Formatting
> 
> Schemas are character-sensitive. Accepted characters are: upper and lower case letters, numbers, underscores, dashes, and spaces.

In each user schema, you can set the following:

Schema property| Description  
---|---  
**Title**|  The property name displayed in the AIRIS console, for example, in reports. This helps users understand the event being tracked, even if they did not participate in the tracking code setup.  
**Description**|  The description is an important reference for the entire team to understand the meaning of the user property, especially those who did not participate in the tracking code setup. It’s always a good practice to add a description to fields to make it easy for other users to take action on this data.  
**Key**|  The key name as tracked on the website. This comes directly from the tracking code. We don't recommend changing this value unless you understand how the events are being sent. If you're unsure, change the **Title** value instead.  
**Property Type**|  The property type can be a standard field, a preset formula, or a custom formula. Refer to [Schema Formulas](doc:schema-formulas) for details.  
**Data Type**|  The data type. One of:  
• **Text** : For any string value.  
• **Number** : For numbers/decimals.  
• **Timestamp** : For dates. Note that these should be sent in milliseconds rather than seconds.  
**Aggregation**|  Specify whether the property is a unique value, amount, or group. Refer to Aggregations for details.  
**Buckets**|  Refer to Buckets for details.  
**Sensitive Information**|  Refer to [Hide Sensitive Data](doc:hide-sensitive-data) for details.  
  
### 

Event schema

The event schema is different from the user schema because it defines the events from the installed integrations or user events tracked on your website or mobile app.

Editing the event schema configuration allows you customize how information is displayed in user [profiles](doc:profiles). For example, instead of “User did event payment”, you could customize the schema to say “ _Jim_ purchased the _Yearly Small Business package_ for _$1,999.50_ “).

#### 

Event info

In each event schema, you can set the following:

Name| Description  
---|---  
**Title**|  The event name displayed in the AIRIS console, for example, in reports. This helps users understand the event being tracked, even if they did not participate in the tracking code setup. Administrators can associate an icon with the event that is being tracked.  
**Description**|  The description is an important reference for the entire team to understand the meaning of this event, especially those who did not participate in the SDK integration process. It’s always a good practice to add a description to fields to make it easy for other agents to act on this data.  
**Key**|  The key name as tracked on the website. This comes directly from the tracking code. We don't recommend changing this value unless you understand how the events are being sent. If you're unsure, change the **Title** value instead.  
**Event Type**|  In [user profiles](doc:profiles#daily-activity-histogram), you can choose whether to show only Active events, only Passive events, or both.  
• **Active** : This event is sent as a result of direct user event.  
• **Passive** : This event is sent as part of a background process or tracked on a user's behalf.  
  
#### 

Event properties

Each property will have the following fields:

Name| Description  
---|---  
**Key**|  The name of the property as it’s tracked e.g. amount.  
**Title**|  The event property name displayed in the AIRIS console, for example, in reports.  
**Description**|  A description of the event property.  
**Data Type**|  The data type of the event property:• **Text** : For any string value.  
• **Number** : For numbers/decimals.  
• **Boolean** : For two possible values. Accepted input is True or False.  
• **Short Boolean** : For two possible values. Acceptable input is 0 for False, 1 for True.  
• **Timestamp** : For dates, e.g. indicating when an event occurred.  
• **Duration** : Specifies a duration in seconds or milliseconds.Only number and timestamp types support the **Format** option, which allows you to specify how the value should be displayed, for example: "$1000.00" or "1000" for numbers, "MM/dd/yyyy" or "dd/MM/yyyy" for timestamps.  
**Aggregate**|  Specify whether the property is a unique value, amount, or group. Refer to Aggregations for details.  
**Buckets**|  Refer to Buckets for details.  
**Formula**|  Define a custom formula to calculate the value of this event property. Refer to [Schema Formulas](doc:schema-formulas) for details.  
**Sensitive Information**|  Refer to [Hide Sensitive Data](doc:hide-sensitive-data) for details.  
**Event Unique ID**|  If enabled, this property is included in the composite ID used for tracking upserts.  
  
##### 

Aggregations

Name| Description  
---|---  
**Group**|  Used for properties that can be applied to multiple events or users, such as company, product, or credit card type.  
**Unique**|  Used for properties that are a unique identifier for a specific event, such as a receipt ID or a transaction ID. For users, a username or email address would be a unique identifier, while company would be a “group” as it can be applied to more than one user.  
**Amount**|  Used for properties that can be added up or summed. When you designate a property as “amount”, you will be able to sum it in your [segmentation filters](doc:segmentation-filters) and analytics reports.For example, in the “payment” event schema, we can set the “amount” property's **Aggregate** setting to **Amount**. Now when we are using segmentation filters, we can segment for “all customers who have made payments that totaled more than $200”. Similarly, our report for the “payment” event will include a column that sums the total payment amounts by day, week, or month.  
  
##### 

Buckets

Buckets can be used to group `number` types for use in trend reports. Press the Enter key to add the next bucket. For example, if a user can purchase different quantities of items from your store, you can use buckets to define each quantity range for use in trend reports.

![](https://files.readme.io/43830b9-Screen_Shot_2024-01-29_at_5.31.38_PM.png)

Now when you run the report, the quantities will be bucketed:

![](https://files.readme.io/aa10cab-Image_2022-11-10_at_3.36.24_PM.jpg)

* * *

## 

Event templates (event and user schemas)

The template is how the event is displayed when it occurs in a timeline or event history. It allows you to insert variables in a sentence that are dynamically populated when viewing an individual user's profile. For example, given a template of "Joined segment `Name`", the `Name` variable would be replaced with the actual segment name when the event is displayed in a user profile.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> See [Templates](doc:woopra-templates) to learn more about creating your own templates.

![](https://files.readme.io/6c02924-action-template.png)

Given the event template shown above, here's how the report entry would look:

![](https://files.readme.io/9336b3d-action-template-in-reports.png)

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Consent Management Platform

Consent management platforms (CMP) are tools that help you collect and manage the cookie consent of your website visitors. Some common consent management platforms include OneTrust, TrustArc, CookieYes, and Osano.

If you are using a CMP, you can pass the consent status of your users to AIRIS. Using AIRIS, you can then use these data to create segments and engage with your users based on the consent granted.

![](https://files.readme.io/447dd43-CookieExample.png)

Follow the instructions below to set up CMP with your website and AIRIS.

  * Part I: Set up cookie categories on CMP
  * Part II: Integrate CMP with the website and AIRIS
  * Part III: Create user schemas on AIRIS
  * Verify integration



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Disclaimer
> 
> This guide uses OneTrust as an example to show how to integrate a CMP with AIRIS. The actual setup steps may be different depending on the CMP you are using. We are not endorsing any CMP and make no guarantee regarding the consent data tracked by the CMP.

* * *

## 

Part I: Set up cookie categories on CMP

On your CMP, you need to set up the different types of cookie consent you want to collect.

You should have a category for necessary cookies, which are cookies required for the website to operate. Depending on your website functionalities and business needs, you might also need to create other non-essential cookie categories. Below is an example of the cookie categories you might have.

  * Necessary cookies
  * Performance cookies
  * Functional cookies
  * Targeting cookies (or advertising cookies)
  * Social media cookies



Here we'll show the interface of OneTrust as an example. Note that each category comes with a **Category ID**. You will need these IDs in later steps.

![](https://files.readme.io/ab29c06-Screen_Shot_2023-01-05_at_9.20.55_AM.png)

The **Category Name** and **Description** of each cookie category will be visible to your website visitor.

![](https://files.readme.io/614ce1c-website_cookie_notice.png)

* * *

## 

Part II: Integrate CMP with the website and AIRIS

Refer to the documentation of your CMP for instructions on how to integrate CMP with your website. In most cases, a code snippet will be provided and you need to add the snippet to your website. You will then need to use the `woopra.identify()` function to send the consent data to AIRIS.

We will use OneTrust as an example. For OneTrust, the easiest way to integrate is to use Google Tag Manager (GTM).

### 

Integrate CMP with your website

To add CMP code snippet to your website, create a variable and create a tag that includes the code snippet on GTM.

  1. Log into your GTM account and go to the container of your website.
  2. In the left menu, click **Variables** and click **New**.

![](https://files.readme.io/10d6662-Variable-new.png)

  3. Name the variable, set the **Variable Type** to **Data Layer Variable** , and enter `OnetrustActiveGroups` under **Data Layer Variable Name**.

![](https://files.readme.io/1010450-Variable-type.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The data layer variable name `OnetrustActiveGroups` is embedded in the OneTrust script and cannot be changed.

  4. Click **Save**.
  5. In the left menu, click **Tags** and click **New** to add the OneTrust code snippet.

![](https://files.readme.io/f0fa824-Tag.png)

  6. Name the tag, set the **Tag Type** to **Custom HTML** , and paste the OneTrust code snippet.

![](https://files.readme.io/a4e9e39-Tag_code_snippet.png)

  7. Under **Triggering** , select **All Pages**. Click **Save**.

![](https://files.readme.io/c508464-Screen_Shot_2023-01-12_at_11.36.42_PM.png)

### 

Send consent data to AIRIS

To send consent data to AIRIS, create a trigger that fires whenever users' consent status is updated and create a tag for `woopra.identify()` to send the data to AIRIS.

  1. In the left menu of GTM, click **Triggers** and click **New**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ba9af9c-Trigger.png)

  2. Name the trigger and set the **Trigger type** to **Custom Event**.

![](https://files.readme.io/8b645a9-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_2.30.26_PM.png)

  3. Set the **Event name** to `OneTrustGroupsUpdated`, select **Use regex matching** and select **All Custom Events**.

![](https://files.readme.io/ddc77ca-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_2.33.29_PM.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> The event name `OneTrustGroupsUpdated` is embedded in the OneTrust script and cannot be changed.

  4. In the left menu, click **Tags** and click **New** to create the tag to send consent data to AIRIS.

![](https://files.readme.io/1c165c8-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_5.59.06_PM.png)

  5. Name the tag, set the **Tag Type** to **Custom HTML** , and add the AIRIS code snippet in the **HTML** field.



JavaScript
    
    
    <script>
      // Place this on a template where a user is initially identified or after authentication
      
      woopra.identify({
        cookie_consent: {{OnetrustActiveGroups}}
                      
      });
    
      // The identify code should be added before the "track()" function
      // If you do not wish to track an event, you can call the function woopra.push();
    
    	woopra.track();
    
    </script>
    

![](https://files.readme.io/cd77e07-Screen_Shot_2023-02-04_at_10.45.37_AM.png)

  6. Under Triggering, select the trigger created above and click **Save**.

![](https://files.readme.io/752726a-Screen_Shot_2023-02-04_at_10.59.32_AM.png)

* * *

## 

Part III: Create user schemas on AIRIS

On AIRIS, create the following user schemas.

  * A user schema called `cookie_consent` that shows the category ID of all consented cookie categories (e.g. `cookie consent: ,C0001,C0002,`).
  * A separate user schema for each cookie category that shows whether the consent status is `true` or `false` (e.g. `Necessary cookies: true`).

![](https://files.readme.io/6f5a7d4-VisitorSchemaList.png)

Follow the steps below.

  1. On AIRIS console, go to **Configure > User Schema** and click **New User Schema**.

![](https://files.readme.io/2151f8d-User_Schema.png)

  2. Name the user schema, set the **Key** name to `cookie_consent`, set **Data Type** to **Text** , and click **Create**. The key name `cookie_consent` must be identical to the user property name used in the `woopra.identify()` function.

![](https://files.readme.io/5cc1f43-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_6.47.45_PM.png)

  3. Click **New User Schema** to create another schema. Now you need to create a user schema for each category to show the consent status of each category in the user profile.

![](https://files.readme.io/1fdc799-User_Schema.png)

  4. Type a user schema name that identifies the cookie category, type a **Key** name, set **Property Type** to **Formula** , and set up the formula like this:
     1. Select **CONTAINS**. The CONTAINS operation returns `true` if the `left` parameter contains the `right` parameter.
     2. For `left` parameter, select the user schema of the cookie consent.
     3. For `right` parameter, type the category ID for the cookie category with a comma before and after the ID (e.g. `,C0001,`).

![](https://files.readme.io/5aa80ad-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_7.06.45_PM.png)

  5. Set **Data Type** to **Boolean** and click **Create**.

![](https://files.readme.io/f7619c4-Screen_Shot_2023-02-02_at_7.08.28_PM.png)

  6. Repeat steps 3 to 5 for each cookie category.

  


* * *

## 

Verify integration

After setup is complete, follow the steps below to check if everything works as expected.

  1. Visit your website and accept the cookie consent.
  2. On AIRIS console, go to **Profiles** and click on your own user profile. The cookie consent status should be consistent which what you have selected.
     * The user's latest consent status can be found under the **Unassigned Properties** section.
     * Changes in cookie consent status can be found in the behavioral feed section.

![](https://files.readme.io/a30f223-Screen_Shot_2023-02-03_at_5.52.54_PM.png)

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> If the consent status is not updated yet, try refreshing the AIRIS console.

  3. This is an optional step. You can organize the consent-related properties into their own section. Start by clicking **Edit Profile Layout**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4c03504-Screen_Shot_2023-02-03_at_5.53.18_PM.png)

Click **New Section** , type a section name such as "🍪 Cookie consent", drag all consent-related properties to that section and click **Save Layout**.

![](https://files.readme.io/6b6e942-Screen_Shot_2023-02-03_at_5.56.26_PM.png)

### 

What can I do with consent data?

You can go to **Configure > Segments** to create segments based on users' consent status. Below is an example of how to create a segment to include users whose consent status for targeting cookies is `true`.

![](https://files.readme.io/1eb8228-Screen_Shot_2023-02-03_at_6.03.08_PM.png)

The segments can be further exported to AIQUA, allowing you to run campaigns according to the consent granted.

__Updated 8 months ago

* * *

# Fields with Special Behaviors

There are a number of user, visit, and event property keys that, if sent as part of the tracking request, will cause some special behavior in the AIRIS UI. For instance, sending `campaign_name` as an event property will actually allow it to be picked up by the system for campaign reporting, and will also show the event's campaign in the interface differently from the rest of the event properties.

Here is a list of fields that have some special behavior by convention:

Field Name| Property Scope| Special Behavior| Discussion  
---|---|---|---  
`url`| Event Property| Parsed for pageview data, campaign data, etc.| AIRIS will read `utm_` and `woo_` tags, and include them as campaign data for the event.  
`campaign_name`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data| AIRIS displays campaign data in each event that has it so as to make attribution clearer. Also, this data when sent with any event, will be used to generate the new Attribution Reports.  
`campaign_id`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data|   
`campaign_source`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data|   
`campaign_medium`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data|   
`campaign_content`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data|   
`campaign_term`| Event Property| Treated as campaign attribution data|

# Copy and Paste Files Within or Between Projects

How to copy and paste reports, schemas, and lists from one project to another.

# 

What can be copied?

Any reports, schemas, or lists such as annotations or segments can be copied and pasted. You can paste copied files into the same project or a different project.

Dashboards, tags, and triggers are not currently supported.

# 

How to Copy and Paste

From the ellipsis (three dots) dropdown, you will see the option to copy a file.

![](https://files.readme.io/4848ae8-Image_2022-08-05_at_4.20.59_PM.jpg)

Once the file is copied, you can paste the file while you're on any AIRIS screen using Command+v on Mac or Ctrl+v on Windows. AIRIS will automatically detect the pasted file.

![](https://files.readme.io/721fd61-Image_2022-08-05_at_4.45.03_PM.jpg)

Once pasted, you'll have a new copy of the report, schema, or list in the currently selected project.

# CSV Loader

![](https://files.readme.io/eea9955-woopra-csv-loader.svg)

## 

Overview

Use data loader's CSV loader to import data from a CSV file into AIRIS.

* * *

## 

1\. Prepare your CSV file

  * For identify imports (attribute data): A unique identifier such as an email address or user ID.
  * For track imports (event data): In addition to a unique identifier, each row of event data must include a timestamp indicating when the event occurred.



### 

Notes and limitations

  * The maximum supported file size depends on the selected **Data Type**:
    * **Identify** , **Track** : 5 GB
    * **Data Store** : 10 MiB
  * Files must be UTF-8 encoded.
  * The file upload timeout is 30 minutes. Import jobs exceeding this duration will result in task failure.
  * To ensure efficient processing of your files, we advise keeping each CSV file under 100,000 rows. If your file contains more than 100,000 rows, we recommend breaking it up into smaller files.
  * Although larger file sizes are technically supported, we don’t recommend uploading files larger than 500 MB to minimize the risk of errors.



* * *

## 

2\. Import your CSV file

To create a new task, go to **Configure** , navigate to **Data Loader > CSV Loader**, then click **Import CSV**.

![](https://files.readme.io/657b3c9-load-csv.png)

  1. Edit CSV name.

![](https://files.readme.io/15480e660d251c8b6460e21ef90717287b16d507217ff05c3c5695d2b8a50184-Name_the_CSV.png)
  2. Enter the URL of the CSV file or upload the file directly from your device.

![](https://files.readme.io/f605763b28400794faa9de7df8a07a18901bd4f2b56cba5ef7eef4aa6f041002-Upload_file.png)   




### 

Enter the URL of the file

To upload a CSV file using a URL, the URL and the file must meet the following requirements:

  * The file must be one of the supported file formats: CSV, TSV.

  * Ensure the URL has public access and the file has not been removed or changed before proceeding to the next step.

  * The URL must include both `sheet_id` and `tab_id`.

    * Example URL: `https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/<sheet_id>/export?format=csv&gid=<tab_id>`
![](https://files.readme.io/4cbfd297c62cf4cc26119da10e79492a7bbcff805f850f7906de26ea87c33eef-Group_1.png)



Next, set the appropriate delimiter:

  * **Comma** : Use this option for CSV files.

  * **Tab** : Use this option for TSV files.

  * **Custom** : Avoid selecting this option for delimiter when uploading a file via URL, as this can lead to errors in processing the file.

![](https://files.readme.io/40d14b2796b878a2195b80a4f8464f26ab86e4a32f96751a0cb78915c62c2add-Group_2.png)



### 

Upload a file from your device

To upload a file from your device, click **Select a file from your device** and set the appropriate delimiter:

  * **Comma** : Use this option for CSV files.
  * **Tab** : Use this option for TSV files.
  * **Custom** : Select this option if your file uses a different delimiter. The appropriate delimiter will depend on the specific file you are uploading.

![](https://files.readme.io/33f3d158851e469213e733195d33835bc219915bc7dafee5fb9bf1c72ebc9be0-Select_file_from_device.png)

  4. Click **Save** once the upload is completed.

![](https://files.readme.io/22f95b2d6a7450445a22e1265017af9aacadf4acf7f0377e14c68e29ad37e61d-image.png)

* * *

## 

3\. Create a task

After importing the CSV file, proceed to create a task to configure the type of data you want to import and how often to check for new data.

To create a new task, go to **Configure** , navigate to **Data Loader > Tasks**, then click **\+ New Task**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b7afe76-create-task.png)

Next, configure the task settings:

  * Source Configurations
  * Map User Events



> ❗️
> 
> ### 
> 
> Confirm task settings
> 
> Profiles may [merge](doc:profile-id-system#airiss-multi-id-system) when tasks import new data. Profile merging is irreversible, so it's critical to ensure tasks are configured correctly before activating them.

### 

Source Configuration

Configure the following settings for your data source:

  * Table
  * Data Type



In addition, you can choose to configure the following optional settings:

  * Conditions
  * Advanced



#### 

Table

Select the table you want to use for importing your data.

  1. Select the data source.
  2. Select the table you want to use.

![](https://files.readme.io/bcebd67-table-setting.png)

#### 

Data Type

Specify the type of data you'll be importing.

  * **Identify** ([user properties](doc:types-of-tracked-data#user-properties)): Identifying information such as company names, emails, first and last names, locations.
  * **Track** ([event data](doc:types-of-tracked-data#event-data)): Anything a user does that contains a timestamp. These can be events like payments, signups, or subscription updates.
  * **Data Store** : Data that will be used to enrich existing data in user profiles.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Track data can also update user properties
> 
> For example, an `Update Profile` event where a user updates their contact information is an event, but the user properties are also being updated.

#### 

Conditions

If you only want to import certain types of data from the table, you can create conditions to filter data by. Rows that satisfy the condition will be imported; rows that don't satisfy the condition won't be imported.

For example, if you're importing payment information and only want to import payment data from the United States, you can create a condition to only import rows where the value in `Country` column is `USA`.

![](https://files.readme.io/41ae90b-Image_2019-11-11_at_3.24.23_PM.png)

#### 

Advanced settings

![](https://files.readme.io/6929d3b-task-advanced-settings.png)

In addition to the settings described above, you can click **Show Advanced** to configure the following advanced settings for CSV files:

  * **Time Zone (optional)** : Select a time zone to automatically convert the timestamp to.
  * **Trigger Support** : Enable this toggle to allow data imported by this task to fire a [trigger](doc:triggers).



Next, create mappings between your data set's columns and the corresponding AIRIS fields.

### 

Map User Events/Map Data to Data Store

Under **Map User Events** (for identify and track data) or **Map Data to Data Store** (for data store data), create the mappings between your data set's columns and the corresponding AIRIS user and event properties. The required mappings differ depending on the type of data you're importing:

  * Data type: Identify
  * Data type: Track
  * Data type: Data Store

![Mapping settings for track \(event\) data.](https://files.readme.io/81faa92-map-user-actions-track.png)

Column mapping settings for track (event) data.

#### 

Data type: Identify

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Unique IDs**|  Specify all the unique IDs contained in the data set.  
**User Properties**|  Map the data set's columns containing user property data, e.g. the user's name, address, or company, to the corresponding user properties in AIRIS profiles.  
**Cursor**|  Not supported for CSV files.  
  
#### 

Data type: Track

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Select & Map User ID**| Set the primary identifier that should be used to map the data to the correct profile, e.g. a user ID or email address.  
**Event name**|  Specify the name of the event for you're importing data, or select an existing event name, e.g. `product_purchased`, `checkout_completed`, `category_viewed`.  
**Event properties**|  Map the columns from the data set containing event property data to the corresponding AIRIS event properties. For example, a `product_purchased` event may contain properties such as `product_name` and `product_id`.  
**User properties**|  Map the columns from the incoming data set with the corresponding user properties.  
**Timestamp**|  Select the column that contains the timestamp of the event. Each event must have a timestamp.  
**Cursor**|  Not supported for CSV files.  
  
#### 

Data type: Data Store

Set mappings between the fields from your data set you'd like to import and the corresponding AIRIS fields.

![](https://files.readme.io/dabd4b9-map-data-to-data-store.png)

* * *

## 

Notes on task behavior

Data loader imports count towards your event quota, so it's crucial to configure tasks correctly to minimize unnecessary reimports and misconfigured data. Please note the following behaviors when deleting tasks or source CSV files:

  * Deleting a task
  * Deleting a source CSV file



### 

Deleting a task

Upon deleting a task:

  * All imported event data is removed.
  * Profiles and properties remain.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> We don't recommend deleting a task unless you want to remove all previously imported event data.

### 

Deleting a source CSV file

Deleting a source CSV file won't have any effect on data that has already been imported into AIRIS, i.e. data that has been imported by a task that has already successfully completed.

However, if a task is in progress and you delete the source CSV file the task is using, the task will fail, and your data may not be imported into AIRIS successfully.

# Data Stores

## 

Overview

Data stores are used to enhance existing data within a user's profile. Any tracked data, whether events or identifying data, can be enriched by augmenting additional data points or modifying existing data.

Some example use cases might include importing ad spend data that you want to tie to specific UTM tags, replacing company IDs with company names, or adding currency codes to specific transactions.

Setting up data augmentation is a three-step process.

  1. Create the data store
  2. Upload a CSV or manually add data
  3. Add new properties in the event or user schema you want to enrich



* * *

## 

Setting up data stores

### 

1\. Create the data store

Start by creating a new data store. The keys and values used for data augmentation will come from the Data Store you create.

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Data stores must not exceed 10 MiB.

  1. To create a new Data Store, navigate to the **Configure** tab, select **Data Stores** under the **Data Loader** section, and click **\+ New Data Store**.

![](https://files.readme.io/81bd74d-Screen_Shot_2023-03-02_at_7.30.06_PM.png)

### 

2\. Upload a CSV or manually add data

Here you can either copy and paste the data into the spreadsheet or click **Import CSV** to upload a CSV with the new data points. Let's say we want to replace company IDs with company names. The CSV will look like this:

![987](https://files.readme.io/338705c-Image_2023-02-24_at_2.35.38_PM.jpg)

One column will be the new company names, and the other will be the company IDs.

  3. Once finished, name your data store and click **Save**.



### 

3\. Add the new property and edit the formula

The third step is to connect the data store to the existing event or user properties.

Sticking with the Company example above, we'll now edit the existing "Company" field in our User Schema. Follow these steps:

![](https://files.readme.io/abb74f2-data-store-visitor-schema.png)

  1. In the user schema for the "Company" field, change the Property Type to **Formula**.

  2. Select **DATASTORE_LOOKUP** in the list of formulas.

  3. Select the data store that you saved, select the **{Company ID}** column in the data store, and then select the matching data field in AIRIS. In this case, select **{THIS}** since we're modifying the existing "Company" field.

  4. Select the new value that we're replacing the Company ID with. In this case, select **{Company name}**.

  5. Select the default value when there are no matches. Again we'll select **{THIS}**.

  6. Lastly, save the new property.




Now you can use this property like you normally would in any report or segment filter. Instead of showing the Company IDs for the entries we added, they will now be replaced with the Company Names.

__Updated about 1 year ago

* * *

[Data Loader Overview](/docs/data-loader-overview)[CSV Loader](/docs/csv-loader)

# Connections

## 

Overview

Use data loader connections to import data from third-party sources into AIRIS.

### 

Requirements

Before adding a connection, you'll need the following:

  * Account or database credentials
  * For identify imports (attribute data): A unique identifier such as an email address or user ID.
  * For track imports (event data): In addition to a unique identifier, each row of event data must include a timestamp indicating when the event occurred.



In addition, if your data source is behind a private subnet, you'll need to add our server IPs to your allowlist so that we can access the data. This also applies to any database or self-hosted database that may be behind a firewall, such as SQL databases (e.g. BigQuery, Redshift, Snowflake).

The list of required IP addresses is listed below. Please add the following IP addresses to your allowlist:

AIRIS IP addresses  
---  
91.134.5.49  
91.134.5.47  
91.134.9.197  
91.134.9.196  
91.134.10.28  
91.134.10.25  
91.134.10.247  
91.134.44.151  
91.134.44.143  
91.134.44.134  
  
  
> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> In addition to allowing all of the AIRIS IP addresses, remember to open the port used by your database

* * *

## 

1\. Create a connection

  1. Navigate to **Configure > Connections**, then click **\+ New Connection**.

![](https://files.readme.io/4f6810a-data-loader-create-new-connection.png)

  2. Select your integration from the dropdown. Please note that the list of connections available in the creation dropdown differs for admin users and non-admin users.



Role| Available connections  
---|---  
**Owner** or **Administrator**|  Users with the **Owner** or **Administrator** role are able to add all connection types.\ ![](https://files.readme.io/85cf4c1-data-loader-connections-admin-view.png)  
**User**|  Users with the **User** role are only able to add personal, user account-based connections, e.g. a connection to a personal Google Drive or Dropbox account.![](https://files.readme.io/832e451-data-loader-connections-user-view.png)  
  
  3. After selecting the integration, enter any required credentials.

![](https://files.readme.io/5b18b24-Image_2019-11-11_at_2.47.26_PM.png)

  4. Save the connection.



* * *

## 

2\. Create a task

After creating a connection, proceed to create a task to configure the type of data you want to import and how often to check for new data.

To create a new task, go to **Configure** , navigate to **Data Loader > Tasks**, then click **\+ New Task**.

![](https://files.readme.io/b7afe76-create-task.png)

Next, configure the task settings:

  * Source Configuration
  * Map User Events



> ❗️
> 
> ### 
> 
> Confirm task settings
> 
> Profiles may [merge](https://docs.airis.appier.com/docs/profile-id-system#airiss-multi-id-system) when tasks import new data. Profile merging is irreversible, so it's critical to ensure tasks are configured correctly before activating them.

* * *

## 

Source Configuration

Configure the following settings for your data source:

  * Table
  * Data Type
  * Conditions
  * Advanced



### 

Table

Select the table you want to use for importing your data.

  1. Select the data source.
  2. Select the table you want to use.

![](https://files.readme.io/eecc742-task-connection-source-configuration.png)

#### 

Optional table joins

It's also possible to import data from multiple tables by configuring the relations or joins for the tables you'll be using. Joining tables allows you to pull in data from multiple tables and tells AIRIS how the tables relate to each other.

If you're using multiple tables, you can define how the tables relate by selecting a common field that's shared by both tables, e.g. an identifier like `email` or `user_id`. This is called a primary/foreign key relationship.

![](https://files.readme.io/677880e-join-table.png)

### 

Data Type

Specify the type of data you'll be importing.

  * **Identify** ([user properties](doc:types-of-tracked-data#user-properties)): Identifying information such as company names, emails, first and last names, locations.
  * **Track** ([event data](doc:types-of-tracked-data#event-data)): Anything a user does that contains a timestamp. These can be events like payments, signups, or subscription updates.
  * **Data Store** : Data that will be used to enrich existing data in user profiles.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Track data can also update user properties
> 
> For example, an `Update Profile` event where a user updates their contact information is an event, but the user properties are also being updated.

### 

Conditions

If you only want to import certain types of data from the table, you can create conditions to filter data by. Rows that satisfy the condition will be imported; rows that don't satisfy the condition won't be imported.

For example, if you're importing payment information and only want to import payment data from the United States, you can create a condition to only import rows where the value in `Country` column is `USA`.

![](https://files.readme.io/41ae90b-Image_2019-11-11_at_3.24.23_PM.png)

### 

Advanced settings

![](https://files.readme.io/3c533ad-task-connection-advanced-settings.png)

In addition to the settings described above, you can click **Show Advanced** to configure the following advanced settings:

Advanced setting| Description  
---|---  
**Sync Interval**|  Specify the wait time between task runs. AIRIS will query the connection for new data based on the sync interval, e.g. if the interval is 10 minutes, AIRIS will query the connection again 10 minutes after the previous task run is completed.Please note that some databases have querying limits, which AIRIS task queries will count towards.  
**Batch Size**|  Specify how many records to batch in one task.  
**Time Zone (optional)**|  Select a time zone to automatically convert timestamp data to.  
**Unique Row Identifier (optional)**|  Select the column containing the row's unique identifier.  
**Trigger Support**|  Enable this toggle to allow data imported by this task to fire a [trigger](doc:triggers).  
  
> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Data store advanced settings
> 
> Note that the **Data Store** data type only supports the **Sync Interval** advanced setting.

Next, create mappings between your data set's columns and the corresponding AIRIS fields.

* * *

## 

Map User Events/Map Data to Data Store

Under **Map User Events** (for identify and track data) or **Map Data to Data Store** (for data store data), create the mappings between your data set's columns and the corresponding AIRIS user and event properties. The required mappings differ depending on the type of data you're importing:

  * Data type: Identify
  * Data type: Track
  * Data type: Data Store

![](https://files.readme.io/d603a07-map-actions.png)

### 

Data type: Identify

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Unique IDs**|  Specify all the unique IDs contained in the data set.  
**User Properties**|  Map the data set's columns containing user property data, e.g. the user's name, address, or company, to the corresponding userproperties in AIRIS profiles.  
**Cursor**|  The cursor is needed to mark the position at which one task run ends and where the next should begin. Once the task starts again after the set sync interval, new data will be retrieved starting from the cursor's position in the data set.For details on selecting an appropriate cursor and the effects of resetting the cursor, see [Cursor Guidelines](doc:suggested-cursor-list).  
  
### 

Data type: Track

Setting| Description  
---|---  
**Select & Map User ID**| Set the primary identifier that should be used to map the data to the correct profile, e.g. a user ID or email address.  
**Event name**|  Specify the name of the event for your importing data, e.g. `product_purchased`, `checkout_completed`, `category_viewed`.  
**Event properties**|  Map the columns from the data set containing event property data to the corresponding AIRIS event properties. For example, a `product_purchased` event may contain properties such as `product_name` and `product_id`.  
**User properties**|  Map the columns from the incoming data set with the corresponding user properties.  
**Timestamp**|  Select the column that contains the timestamp of the event. Each event must have a timestamp.  
**Cursor**|  The cursor is needed to mark the position at which one task run ends and where the next should begin. Once the task starts again after the set sync interval, new data will be retrieved starting from the cursor's position in the data set.For details on selecting an appropriate cursor, see [Cursor Guidelines](doc:suggested-cursor-list).  
  
### 

Data type: Data Store

Set mappings between the fields from your data set you'd like to import and the corresponding AIRIS fields.

![](https://files.readme.io/2293124-map-data-to-data-store.png)

* * *

## 

Notes on task behavior

Data loader imports count towards your event quota, so it's crucial to configure tasks correctly to minimize unnecessary reimports and misconfigured data. Please note the following behaviors when deleting, resetting, or modifying existing tasks:

  * Pausing a task
  * Deleting a connection



### 

Pausing a task

Pausing a task pauses the import process. When the task is resumed, the import will continue starting from where it left off according to the cursor's last position. Note that when a paused task is modified:

  * Changes will take effect when the task is resumed again.
  * Changes will only apply to data imported after resuming the task. To apply changes to all previously imported data, you'll need to reset the cursor and reimport all previous data.



### 

Deleting a connection

Upon deleting a connection:

  * All associated tasks are deleted.
  * All imported event data is removed.
  * Profiles and properties remain.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> We don't recommend deleting a connection unless you want to remove all previously imported event data and the connection's associated tasks.

__Updated 2 months ago

* * *

[CSV Loader](/docs/csv-loader)[Tasks](/docs/data-loader-tasks)

# Data Loader Overview

## 

Overview

AIRIS's data loader offers the following features and benefits:

  * **Data integrity** : Relying on frontend or client-side tracking can often lose data due to various factors such as blockers or incorrect coding. Use AIRIS's data loader to establish a direct connection to your data source and maintain lossless data from your database or data source.
  * **Historical data** : While event-based tracking records data in real-time, there's no way to access historical data unless it was recorded previously. The data loader allows you import your historical data and make it available for immediate consumption and analysis.
  * **No developer required** : With traditional event tracking, you usually need a developer to code custom events on your website. With AIRIS's data loader, all you need is database credentials to start importing data. Setup is easy, and you can simply select what data you want to bring in — no coding required.



> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Data retention
> 
> AIRIS supports a minimum data retention period of 2 years. To increase your organization's data retention period, contact your customer success manager.

* * *

## 

Using the AIRIS data loader

You can import data into AIRIS using the data loader in three different ways:

  * [CSV Loader](doc:csv-loader): Import a CSV directly from the AIRIS console.
  * [Connections](doc:data-loader-connections): Use one of AIRIS's third-party service integrations to import data to AIRIS.
  * [Data stores](doc:data-augmentation-data-stores): Use data imported from a CSV or connection to enrich existing AIRIS user profiles.

* * *

# Tasks

## 

Overview

AIRIS tasks allow you to select an existing data source, e.g. data from a third-party service or a CSV file, and allow you to configure:

  * What type of data you want to import
  * How often to check for new data
  * How the import data should map to your AIRIS data



### 

Requirements

Before creating a task, you'll need to add your data source. You can import data into AIRIS using the data loader in three different ways:

  * [CSV Loader](doc:csv-loader)
  * [Connections](doc:data-loader-connections)
  * [Data Stores](doc:data-augmentation-data-stores)



* * *

## 

Creating a task

To create a task, navigate to the **Configure** section, go to **Data Loader > Tasks**, then click **\+ New Task**.

![](https://files.readme.io/73c3884-create-task.png)

Next, configure settings for source configurations and data mapping.

  * **Source configurations** : Set the data source, table, and data type for this import task.
  * **Data mapping** : Create mappings between imported data fields and AIRIS fields.

![](https://files.readme.io/ea3534a-task-settings.png)

Note that the available settings may differ depending on the type of data source you're using, e.g. third-party connection or CSV file. For detailed instructions, refer to the guide for the data source you're using:

  * [CSV Loader](doc:csv-loader)
  * [Connections](doc:data-loader-connections)
  * [Data Store](doc:data-augmentation-data-stores)

# Drilldown Reporting

When creating various reports, you can drill down or filter on various data points in your reports. You can drill down to any report from Journeys, Trends, People, and Retention Reports.

# 

Drilldown Reports

### 

Journey to Trends Example

You can click on a data point in the Journey and drill down to a new report. We can choose from any of our existing reports. Here, we're drilling down to a Trends Report on the 61 people that completed step 3 in our Journey.

![](https://files.readme.io/c2d53de-Image_2020-05-20_at_5.39.36_PM.png)

After you drill down to the new Trend Report, the new report is segmented on those 61 people. We can also edit the Trend Report by adding additional "Compare By" filters to break down the data even further.

![](https://files.readme.io/0df5b0a-Image_2020-05-20_at_5.41.47_PM.png)

# 

Filtering Reports

Instead of drilling down to a new report, we can also filter the current report based on the data point. Let's take a look at that same Journey Report.

![](https://files.readme.io/f423ea4-Image_2020-05-20_at_5.44.29_PM.png)

As you can see, we have 61 people who "ran a report" in step 3 in our Journey. Now I can filter this same report based on those 61 people.

![](https://files.readme.io/8510573-Image_2020-05-20_at_5.45.57_PM.png)

Once you filter the report, you can now see that step 3 in the Journey has a 100% conversion. The report is now being filtered by the 61 people from that step. Essentially, those 61 people are now being used as a segment (or Performed By) filter.

![](https://files.readme.io/b31faa0-Image_2020-05-20_at_5.50.32_PM.png)

These are just a few examples of the possibilities with drill down reporting. Try experimenting to find deeper insight into your data!

__Updated about 1 year ago

# Drupal

  1. Login to your site.
  2. Click Administer -> Site Building -> Blocks -> Add Block.
  3. Give the new block a Block Description of AIRIS. Leave the title blank.
  4. Copy the JavaScript snippet provided in the JavaScript app in AppConnect.
  5. Just below the Block Body, click the Input Format, and set it to PHP code.



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> If this option is not available, cancel and make sure you have the PHP filter enabled in your modules section and then start again.

  6. Scroll down and click Save Block.
  7. In the list of Blocks you should now see AIRIS and the Region set to None.
  8. Set the Region to be whatever is closest to the header. Some themes have a left/right/center/header/footer. Any placement should work but the closer to the header, the better.
  9. Scroll down and click Save Blocks.
  10. Once the code is installed, you can view your site’s stats by visiting from an up-to-date browser. AIRIS works best in Chrome and Safari but FireFox works as well.

# eCommerce Tracking

## 

The Ultimate Event Tracking Setup Guide: eCommerce Edition

We realize that every industry and vertical is unique, for example, an eCommerce company will usually track different events than a mobile gaming or travel company. To help guide you through this process, we’ve compiled tips and best practices by industry to provide **the Ultimate Tracking Setup Guide: eCommerce Edition.**

### 

Identifying key customer lifecycle moments for eCommerce

Unlike traditional retail stores, eCommerce establishments possess a unique advantage—the capability to track and customer actions. By precisely determining which events to track and specifying the details to accompany each event, you can gather the necessary information to generate valuable insights into your users.

If you’re using [WooCommerce](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/woocommerce) or [Shopify](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/shopify), you can streamline this process by installing the integration with a few clicks. This will automatically begin tracking relevant eCommerce platform events right out of the box! But what if you’re not using one of these integrations?

We’ve combed through the most successful eCommerce companies using AIRIS and identified trends in what they’re tracking to provide you with a foundation for getting started. Once user property and event logging is set up, the information will display on the user’s AIRIS profile and you can begin to segment, build customer journeys, and leverage the data in your reporting!

> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> Keep in mind that the help of a developer will be required to code the custom events you’d like to track. By using this guide, you’ll be able to outline the key events first to **significantly cut down on implementation and development time!**

* * *

## 

Tracking marketing website events

### 

Anonymous website traffic

AIRIS is designed to track unique, unidentified website and mobile app users from their first touch. The user is assigned a unique ID and all of their anonymous activity is tracked in the customer profile. You’ll also have data regarding the user’s location, system, platform, referrer, and more!

Once the user is identified — by logging their details during a sign up or subscription, for example — all previous anonymous activity will be merged into the same [profile](doc:profiles). This provides a complete picture of the full lifecycle for every user, starting with the first touch.

To start tracking anonymous website traffic, complete the Appier Web SDK integration (see [Tracking Overview](doc:tracking-overview)) and ensure that the SDK code is pasted into every page of your site that you’d like to track.

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> If you’re hosting your website on WordPress, you don’t need to install the Javascript snippet to track website activity. Simply, install the [Woopra WordPress integration](https://wordpress.org/plugins/woopra/) and the tracking code will automatically be added!

### 

Additional page view events

Most of the activity on your site likely happens pre-sale. That’s when customers are exploring your site, looking through your products, and managing their carts. All of this activity needs to be tracked. Remember that, along with the events, you'll also want to send properties, which are pieces of information that describe the event. Here are some of the key events and properties you should be sent outside of basic page views.

### 

Product page view

You’ll want to track when website users are viewing specific products, product categories, and product pages on your site. Along with the page view event, we suggest tracking:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_id`
  * `product_category`
  * `product_url`



> 📘
> 
> ### 
> 
> Note
> 
> This event should only replace the default page view event in product pages.

Here’s an example of the tracking code that you could customize to track product view events:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'product_id': 'E0238',
      'product_name': 'Brand A Computer',
      'product_category': 'electronics',
      'product_url': 'https://example.com/products/brand-a-computer'
    }
    appier('event', 'product_viewed', event_parameters);

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Adding the product URL to a product viewed event will generate a link to the product in the AIRIS user profile. This is an easy way to quickly reference the exact product the user viewed.

### 

Product video view

Tracking when a user plays product videos helps identify the importance of content on conversions. The properties to send with this event might include:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_id`
  * `product_category`
  * `product_url`
  * `video_url`



When you customize the code, it should look similar to this:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'product_id': 'E0238',
      'product_name': 'Brand A Computer',
      'category': 'electronics',
      'product_url': 'https://mystore.com/products/brand-a-computer',
      'video_url': 'https://mystore.com/products/brand-a-computer#play-video'
    }
    appier('event', 'video_viewed', event_parameters);

### 

Blog view or comment

How do you know what types of content perform better than others? Are users who read a blog post more likely to convert to paying customers?

Track your blog activity to measure blog attribution, inform future blog post topics, and measure engagement. You can leverage this data in reporting and funnels to optimize engagement.

For example, the following "Blog Attribution" funnel shows how many people read a blog post and then continue to take other actions on the website. You can see how many users read a post and subsequently subscribed, added an item to their cart, added a credit card, and made a purchase.

![](https://files.readme.io/a1e190a-Blog-Journey-Ecommerce.png)

To track your blog activity, provide the following example to your developer.

JavaScript
    
    
    appier('event', 'article_viewed', {
      'title': 'Blog post title',
      'url': 'https://wwww.blog.example.com/',
      'author': 'Leon Kimura',
      'category': 'News,
      'published_on': 125234234234 // Timestamp indicating when the post was published
    });
    
    // You can also log an event when a user leaves a comment
    appier('event', 'article_commented', {
      'comment': 'Great article!'
    });

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Check out [this article](http://www.marketingprofs.com/articles/2016/31239/how-to-use-customer-insight-to-create-better-content?adref=nlt120816&utm_content=45777337&utm_medium=social&utm_source=twitter) to learn how to leverage customer insight to fuel content decisions!

### 

Contact form submission

It’s important to track when users identify themselves and engage with other content on your site. For eCommerce, this might include contact forms for:

  * Providing feedback
  * Submitting a complaint
  * Order status inquiries



For customer success teams, this information can help you measure the effectiveness of your efforts and allow you to optimize areas for improvement. You can also tie these efforts directly back to ROI by measuring how many users submit support inquiries or provide feedback and make (or don’t make) a purchase.

To track contact form submissions, ask your developer to customize the code snippet below for each of your contact forms:

JavaScript
    
    
    // Log the user's properties
    const user_properties = {
      'email': '[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)',
      'first_name': 'Foo',
      'last_name': 'Bar'
    }
    appier('identify', user_properties);
    
    // Log the feedback event
    appier('event', 'feedback_form', {
      'subject': 'Support feedback',
      'message': 'The live chat support was very helpful',
    });

### 

Website video play

If you have a promotional video on your homepage or landing pages throughout your site, you’ll want to track when users play your video content. This is separate from product video views as they do not directly relate to a specific product. Customize the following code sample to begin sending and measuring video engagement data in AIRIS:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'title': 'Video title',
      'videoId': '1SF3sx',
      'url': 'https://www.example.com/promo-video'
    }
    appier('event', 'video_played', event_parameters);

### 

Product added to cart

Tracking when users add items to their shopping cart allows for greater personalization and insights in your AIRIS reports. For example, if you send the `product_id` along with the add to cart event, you could trigger an email campaign to everyone who added that specific item to their cart. The properties you could include with the `product_added_to_cart` event could include:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_price`
  * `product_category`
  * `product_url`
  * `quantity` (if the user is removing an item from the cart, the quantity will be negative)



In addition, the final positional parameter specifies the monetary value associated with the event—in this case, it represents the product's price. After deciding which properties to send along with the `product_added_to_cart` event, your code could look like this:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'product_id': 'A5343S',
      'product_name': 'Brand A Phone',
      'product_category': 'Mobile devices & accessories',
      'product_url': 'https://www.example.com/brand-a-phone',
      'quantity': 1
    }
    appier('event', 'product_added_to_cart', event_parameters, 799.99);

### 

Shipping estimate

After customers add items to their cart, you’ll probably want to provide them with an estimate of their shipping costs. Tracking this event can show if the displayed shipping price impacts conversion rates. Properties to send along with shipping estimate events include:

  * `zip_code`
  * `shipping_price`



Your code snippet will look similar to the following example:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'zip_code': 94104,
      'shipping_price': 12.95
    }
    
    appier('event', 'estimate_shipping', event_parameters)

### 

Product review read

If customers leave reviews for the products that you’re selling, it’s helpful to know who reads the reviews. This will allow you to measure if the product reviews negatively or positively impact conversion rates. Some important properties to track along with this event include the:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_id`
  * `product_category`
  * `product_url`



You also could track:

  * `total_reviews`: This can help you to understand if people are seeing enough reviews for the product they wish to purchase.
  * `rating`: This can help show how ratings contribute to conversions.



In addition, the final positional parameter specifies the monetary value associated with the event—in this case, it represents the product's price. Here’s an example of what your custom code snippet could look like when including the above properties:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'product_name': 'Brand A Phone',
      'product_id': 'A5343S',
      'product_url': 'https://www.example.com/brand-a-phone',
      'product_category': 'Mobile devices & accessories',
      'total_reviews': 412,
      'review_rating': 4.9
    }
    
    appier('event', 'read_product_review', event_parameters, 799.99)

### 

Product review submission

Online reviews are essential. [92 percent of consumers](https://www.vendasta.com/blog/50-stats-you-need-to-know-about-online-reviews) now rely on product reviews to help inform purchasing decisions. If you offer reviews on your products, you’ll want to track when customers submit reviews to analyze how they impact product purchases. Properties to pass with this event include:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_id`
  * `product_url`
  * `product_category`
  * `review_number`
  * `review_url`
  * `review_rating`



In addition, the final positional parameter specifies the monetary value associated with the event—in this case, it represents the product's price. Once you’ve decided which properties matter most to you, customize the code snippet like the example below:

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'product_name': 'Brand A Phone',
      'product_id': 'A5343S',
      'product_url': 'https://www.example.com/brand-a-phone',
      'product_category': 'Mobile devices & accessories',
      'review_url': 'https://mystore.com/products/iphone-7-plus/reviews/412',
      'review_number': 413,
      'review_rating': 5
    }
    
    appier('event', 'product_reviewed', event_parameters, 799.99)

### 

Tracking campaigns with UTM tags

If you spend a significant amount of time posting to social media, running AdWords campaigns or sharing content – you’re driving traffic from hundreds of different URLs a day. But, how do you know which social media posts drove the most traffic? Can you quickly see which campaigns drove the most conversions over time?

Using UTM parameters, you can compare the performance of campaigns from different mediums and channels and measure over time. A UTM tag will answer questions such as:

  * Where are my users coming from?
  * How are they finding me?
  * What happens after they engage with my campaign?



Once you've integrated with the Appier SDK, AIRIS will automatically begin tracking UTM tags for you. You can then set up journeys and trends reports to monitor and measure campaign effectiveness by factors such as campaign name, source, content.

For example, the following journey report shows how many users came to AIRIS through a campaign, sorted by campaign name, and the subsequent actions they took.

![](https://files.readme.io/277cb8e-Campaign-Conversions-ecommerce.png)

### 

Product search

Users searching on your website can offer insight into popular products, product categories, and frequently asked questions. Leveraging this data will allow you to personalize their experience and provide the most relevant experience possible. Customize the below tracking snippet to send product search events from your website to AIRIS and include properties such as:

  * `keyword`
  * `total_results`



The total number of search results allows you to find gaps in product offerings and understand if users are unable to find products they’d like to purchase.

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'keyword': 'phone',
      'total_results': 12
    }
    
    appier('event', 'product_search', event_parameters)

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Including the number of search results will allow you to segment searches where users didn’t find any results. This tells you where there might be gaps in your content. It’s also an opportunity to reach out directly and provide an answer or automate an email campaign to provide additional assistance.

### 

Live chat

One of your main objectives is to get the user to identify themselves on your marketing website. A common strategy is to provide a frictionless, live chat feature.

If you’re using one of [AIRIS’s integrations partners](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/) for chat, simply navigate to Integrations in your AIRIS instance and install the integration. If not, see the below code snippet as an example of how to track live chat events.

JavaScript
    
    
    // Log the user properties
    const user_properties = {
      'first_name': 'First name',
      'email': '[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)',
      'phone': '123456789' 
    }
    appier('identify', user_properties);
    
    // Log the event
    appier('event', 'start_chat');

### 

Sign-ups

Every eCommerce company should track customer and user sign-ups. They’re a crucial indicator of traction and allow you to nurture customer relationships over time. For example, you should measure how many people sign up to make a purchase on your website vs. how many continue to checkout as a guest. Properties that you can send along with a sign-up event might include:

  * `email`
  * `name`
  * `user_id`
  * `address` (the shipping address)



As well as any other fields relevant to your business that you’re collecting during sign-ups. For example, a clothing store might also want to include a user’s size details and any personal preferences.

The most important property to send with the signup event is the user’s email address. This is what allows you to identify the user and track them across multiple devices (e.g. laptop, phone, work computer).

To track these signup events, you can define the event name string and the properties associated with that event object, as shown below. The following code will track a custom `signup` event with additional information about the new account.

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'name': 'Isabella Kim',
      'user_id': 'isabella_kim',
      'email': '[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)',
      'address': 'XYZ Street',
    }
    appier('event', 'signup', event_parameters);

Passing these events into AIRIS will automatically show the information being sent in real-time on the user profiles and you can start to run custom reporting and funnels. For example, you could measure the number of sign ups by location to understand if your brand is gaining more traction in one area of the world than another.

### 

Login

How often do users login to your application? Who is logging on and what actions do they take next? Tracking login events will answer these questions and help you understand factors, such as the rate at which users successfully log in and the authentication methods used.

Having this data gives you the ability to calculate conversions and identify your most engaged customers. Work with your developer to have log in events sent to AIRIS. Here’s an example of what the code will look like:

JavaScript
    
    
    // Send at least the email address to AIRIS. This will allow the user
    // to be identified even if they're logging in from a different device.
    appier('identify', {'email': '[[email protected]](/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection)'})
    appier('event', 'login');

### 

Profile update

When a user updates their profile information on your website, you’ll want to track this information in AIRIS. Send it as a `profile_update` event, and include any other parameters that are relevant to your user profiles, e.g. mailing address, email, or when they update their preferred payment method.

JavaScript
    
    
    const user_properties = {
      'first_name': 'First name',
      'last_name': 'Last name'
    }
    
    appier('identify', user_properties);
    appier('event', 'profile_update', user_properties);

### 

Add payment method

See how many users add a payment method before checkout by tracking an "add payment method" event. Be sure to include the payment type as a property to understand which payment methods are the most popular!

JavaScript
    
    
    appier('event', 'add_payment_method',{'payment_type': 'Visa'});

### 

Discounts and promotions

Understand which promotions are used the most frequently to optimize promotional campaigns and measure their success! Include the below properties with your event:

  * `coupon_code`
  * `valid` (true or false)



JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'coupon_code': 'SUMMER24',
      'valid': true,
    }
    appier('event', 'apply_coupon', event_parameters);

### 

Checkout

Whenever a user checks out, you’ll want to send properties such as payment amount and the product being checked out. Properties to include along with this event include:

  * `total_items`
  * `discount_amount`
  * `tax_amount`
  * `shipping_amount`
  * `order_id`



In addition, the final positional parameter specifies the monetary value associated with the event—in this case, it represents the product's price.

JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'total_items': 3,
      'discount_amount': 0,
      'tax_amount': 154.23,
      'shipping_amount': 12.95,
      'order_id': '22bksmkdz4m9ds',
    }
    appier('event', 'checkout_completed', event_parameters, 1430.43);

### 

Itemized checkout

When tracking customer checkouts, you should also track the itemized checkout to understand the actual sales for every product. For example, if the checkout includes one phone and two phone cases, you’ll want to track each of those items separately so that you can study sales by product item and category. Include event properties such as:

  * `product_name`
  * `product_id`
  * `product_category`
  * `product_url`
  * `quantity`



In addition, the final positional parameter specifies the monetary value associated with the event—in this case, it represents the product's price.

JavaScript
    
    
    // Log an event for first product
    let event_parameters = {
      'product_id': 'A5343S',
      'product_name': 'Brand A Phone',
      'product_category': 'Mobile devices & accessories',
      'product_url': 'https://www.example.com/brand-a-phone',
      'quantity': 1
    }
    appier('event', 'product_purchased', event_parameters, 799.99);
    
    // Log an event for the second product
    event_parameters = {
      'product_id': 'B4723CV',
      'product_name': 'Phone case',
      'product_category': 'Mobile devices & accessories',
      'product_url': 'https://www.example.com/phone-case',
      'quantity': 2
    }
    appier('event', 'product_purchased', event_parameters, 49.95);

> 👍
> 
> ### 
> 
> Tip
> 
> Do you use Stripe for payment processing? Even better! We’ve built a custom integration with [Stripe](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/stripe) to make payment tracking seamless. Simply install the integration and let AIRIS do the work!

### 

Subscriptions

If you’re an eCommerce company with a subscription model, it’s important to measure new subscriptions, and it’s equally important to measure subscription changes over time to keep a pulse on the health of your customer base. You can drill down based on any available metrics such as geolocation, campaigns, lead sources, and platforms. Tracking subscription events in AIRIS will allow you to answer essential questions such as:

  * What are my highest and lowest performing customer segments?
  * At what point are customers likely to churn?
  * How often do people engage with my product (or take any action) after becoming a customer?



Every time a customer subscription is created, modified, or canceled, you can send a `subscription_update` event to AIRIS with the following properties:

  * `old_acv`: The previous ACV value of the subscription.
  * `new_acv`: The new ACV value of the subscription.
  * `delta`: The difference between previous ACV and new ACV.



For example, if a customer upgrades from a $99/month (ACV $1,188) package to a $199/month (ACV $2,388) package, the resulting increase in your ARR (Annual Recurring Revenue) is your ACV delta, would be $1,200.

To begin tracking subscription events, follow the same steps to define your event name string and the properties associated with the event object. Adding the above-mentioned properties, your code will look like this:

JavaScript
    
    
    appier('event', 'subscription_update', {
      'old_acv': 1188.0,
      'new_acv': 2388.0,
      'delta': 1200.0
    });

  * If the customer is creating the subscription for the first time, the old ACV is 0
  * If the customer is downgrading the subscription, the old ACV is higher than new ACV and the delta is negative
  * If the customer is canceling their subscription, the new ACV is 0 and the delta is negative



### 

Errors and issues

Whether we like it or not, issues and problems occur! By tracking the errors that users encounter, you can arm your customer success team with data to help them provide better service and support. For example, you can send the following events for the various form errors a user may encounter:

JavaScript
    
    
    appier('event', 'form_error', {
      'form': 'signup form',
      'error': 'Email already registered'
    });
    
    appier('event', 'form_error', {
      'form': 'login form',
      'error': 'Wrong username or password'
    });
    
    appier('event', 'form_error', {
      'app_version': '1.2',
      'error_code': 500,
      'error': 'Failed to download resource'
    });
    

* * *

## 

Tracking server-side events

Server-side events can be useful especially when those events are performed on behalf of the user. Here are some events that you should consider tracking.

### 

Order shipped

It’s important to track when a customer's order has successfully shipped, as you can leverage this data to automate email campaigns when the item has arrived and to assist customers experiencing issues. Include properties such as:

  * `order_id`
  * `shipping_method`
  * `days` (days until delivered)



JavaScript
    
    
    const event_parameters = {
      'order_id': '22bksmkdz4m9ds',
      'shipping_method': 'FedEx',
      'days': 5
    }
    appier('event', 'product_shipped', event_parameters);

### 

Refunds

Track when an item is refunded, returned, or exchanged. Be sure to include the `refund_amount` event parameter as shown in the following example:

JavaScript
    
    
    appier('event', 'issue_refund', {'refund_amount': 344.95});

### 

Email events

It’s important for marketers, sales and customer support teams to track when users open, receive and click on emails. AIRIS integrates with most of the popular email marketing platforms to make email tracking a breeze. Track email events and update lead lists automatically!

For example, the [Marketo app](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/marketo) will track Marketo events such as an email is sent, or when a user opens and clicks on it. These events are tracked, sent to AIRIS, and included in the AIRIS profile.

In addition, you’ll have [trend reports](doc:trends) reflecting these events. For example, you may want to analyze which emails had the most opens among customers in California. Or, you may want to build a [journey report](doc:journeys-v3) report showing where people drop off on the path to conversion after they come from an email campaign – and ultimately what impact their email marketing is having on conversion.

You can also set up [automations](doc:triggers) to automatically send new leads to your email marketing software or update existing lead records. For example, if someone signs up on your website, you can set up a trigger to automatically send the lead’s information to Hubspot and a new Hubspot Lead will be created. If they change their company information, later on, those fields will be updated in their Hubspot Lead record.

Want to use all this data in AIRIS to update your email marketing lists based on user behavior? This functionality is also built into our integrations. For example, you could update your MailChimp List for “Ready to Buy” users whenever a user adds an item to their cart, enters their shipping information and adds a credit card to their profile!

Pick your email platform below to learn more about each integration:

  * [Marketo](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/marketo)
  * [Hubspot](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/hubspot)
  * [MailChimp](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/mailchimp)
  * [SendGrid](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/sendgrid)
  * [Campaign Monitor](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/campaignmonitor)
  * [Customer.io](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/customerio)



### 

Support tickets

Are users who submit support requests more likely or less to convert? What impact do certain product features have on support tickets? Do well-answered support tickets drive conversions? Find all of this out and more by [integrating](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations) with one of our customer support tools!

Track when users submit tickets and track when they get resolved. This is helpful to study the attribution of your customer success team to the retention of your customers.

### 

NPS score events

Are you collecting valuable NPS data from your customers? Track this data in AIRIS to build audiences of promoters and detractors. Integrating with [Delighted](https://airis.appier.com/project/~/configure/integrations/delighted) will allow you to automatically see NPS survey results on profiles on AIRIS. You can trigger personalized emails to re-engage detractors or send a special offer to your promoters!
